  • Scope: the attention used in Llama-3.1-8B training. Op shape b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16, causal (bottom-right; equivalent to top-left when Sq=Skv), BSHD, GQA 4. The e2e setup is Llama-3.1-8B with 32 layers (some 09-15/16 experiments used 8 layers), BF16, single card, MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192 (32,768 tokens per step), AC none, torch.compile off.
  • FLOP convention: the current convention is op-evolve's tools/op_flops.py: per call, fwd 2.199292e12 and bwd 5.498229e12 (5-GEMM nominal count), fwd+bwd 7.697e12. The 09-13..09-16 documents report only the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 ÷ total time); this report does not mix in other conventions such as the 7-GEMM issued count or the old harness's fwd ≈2.233e12; see Appendix A.3.
  • Data cutoff: the last measurement is the product-branch e2e tutorial of 2026-10-05 ((d) also records a wedge in another project on 10-06, as a risk reference only). This report was written on 2026-10-07 and revised once the same day after a completeness review (the pre-review full text is kept in _work/SUMMARY.v1.md).
  • Disclaimer: this report only compiles existing records (the daily reports under output/, the op-evolve ledgers, session logs, git history); no GPU job was run for it. A0's GPU is currently in use by a colleague.
  • Files (all under Primus-Turbo/output/1007__summary/):
    • SUMMARY.en.md: this report (English version), final; where it disagrees with any English file below, SUMMARY.en.md prevails.
    • rounds.en.xlsx: per-round details, one row per round (198 rows, none merged). Sheet 1 "All rounds" holds all the details, sheet 2 "Improvement rounds" separately lists the rounds that brought an improvement and the size of each improvement, and sheet 3 "Legend" explains the columns, the machine codes and the colors. The data source is parts/en/rounds.en.csv.
    • REPORT-1007.en.html: the HTML version of this report, self-contained; just open it in a browser.
    • Chinese version: SUMMARY.md, REPORT-1007.html, rounds.xlsx (the originals), which correspond one-to-one with the English version (the English sources are in parts/en/); where the two disagree, the Chinese version is authoritative.
    • parts/: section drafts. SUMMARY.md is the direct concatenation, in this order, of 0_head.md (title, notes, one-page conclusions, timeline), a_mainline.md, b_backends.md, c_rounds.md, d_problems.md and z_appendix.md (Appendix A/B/C); rounds.csv is the data source of rounds.xlsx (including columns such as stage, whether improved, and improvement size); en/ holds the corresponding English source files. On content, SUMMARY.md prevails.
    • _work/: scratch area: session text exports (sessions/), intermediate files and pre-edit backups of each subtask (scratch/), the v1 section drafts (v1/), the pre-review full text SUMMARY.v1.md, and the session-export scripts (tools/). It can be deleted as a whole without affecting the files above (_work/.gitignore already ignores all of its contents).
    • tools/: build.sh concatenates parts/ into SUMMARY.md / SUMMARY.en.md and generates the two HTML files and the two xlsx files (CPU only); md2html.py is the HTML conversion script and build_xlsx.py generates the xlsx files. After editing parts/, just run tools/build.sh.
  • Notation: A0 = heliosr-1b114-c07-1 (this machine, single card); B0 = ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1 (4 cards). Before its 09-29 firmware reflash, A0 was under a VR throttle (sclk capped at 1100 MHz); A0's absolute numbers from before 09-29 can be compared neither with later A0 numbers nor with B0. The four sections use different names for the same clock phase (A0-VR / E1 / A0-T / A0-throttled, etc.); see Appendix A.1 for the mapping. All times are UTC.

0. One-page conclusions

  1. Starting point: unmodified main does not run on gfx1250 by default. On upstream main c1325c7e (#481), with torch 2.11 it crashes on import (register_opaque_type); default dispatch lands on CK, which supports only CDNA (without aiter in the image, the very first step hits ImportError; with aiter installed, bwd fails mid-training with invalid argument for fmha_bwd); all tests on gfx1250 are skipped. The only backend that can be pinned by hand, Triton, copies CDNA's single config: on A0-VR, fwd+bwd 58.424 ms / 131.7 TF/s (bench0910, total convention; 59.642 ms / 129.0 on the branch tree's tune ruler), ~1.9× slower than flex, with the dkdv kernel alone taking 61.5%. On the e2e side, A0 had never completed a single step before; once it ran on 09-13 it reached only 245 tok/s, 133.7 s/step (GEMM fell back to rocBLAS, 27.4 TF/s), and converters: [] meant that turbo attention never even entered e2e. Details in (a).
  2. Backends after adaptation (compared only within the same clock phase). The A0-VR 09-13 bake-off (fwd+bwd total, fastest first): aiter Triton with 2 knobs 31.207 ms, flex 31.337, aiter Triton factory default 34.565, turbo Triton tuned 36.405, turbo Triton factory default 59.642, SDPA FLASH 97.063. The best Triton-family bwd takes about 2–2.4 times as long as ASM (≈, across rulers: the Triton figure is a reading from E1 tune_attention.py, whose timing includes the autograd plumbing; ASM is taken as the E3 benchmark.py bar of 7.68 ms, or as the slower same-process bring-up reading of 8.661 ms), and the best fwd about 1.5 times (≈, B0 09-14); this is a structural gap (a single ASM WG uses the full 320 KiB of LDS and 1024 VGPRs). aiter's precompiled ASM is the fastest ready-made implementation: on A0-VR, fwd 1.5724 ms / 1398.67 TF/s and bwd 7.6766 ms / ≈716 TF/s (09-24 census); after A0's firmware reflash, fwd 1.257 ms / 1750 and bwd 5.503 ms / 999 TF/s (09-30, blocked ruler, randn). Details in (b).
  3. The fastest implementation, ASM, cannot be used as is. bwd has no Python entry point for gfx1250 (can_impl_fmha_v3_bwd does not recognize gfx1250), so a ~674-line launcher had to be written by hand; under GQA it writes dk/dv out of bounds per q head (at ratio=4, dk −0.94 / dv −0.65 dB); the workaround is dkdv_heads=q plus a host-side reduction, which costs 1.254 GiB more peak memory and 0.482 ms more in backward (A0-VR, +5.2%); only 52 .co files were released for gfx1250 (gfx950 has 1466), only 6 of them bwd, and no varlen; dq uses fp32 atomic accumulation, so results are nondeterministic; one call takes 3 kernels and 6 dispatches; aiter pins flydsl 0.3.2 and Primus-Turbo pins 0.2.4, so the two cannot share a process and ASM cannot become an upstream product backend. In addition, the ASM bwd 10.160 ms / 541 TF/s measured on 09-15 was an artifact of the autograd shim and was not retracted until 09-24; until then, FlyDSL's progress had been reported as 0.92× of ASM when it was actually 0.70–0.72×. These problems are the reasons for the 09-17 decision to develop in-house FlyDSL kernels.
  4. FlyDSL bwd: from 1/12.6 of ASM to 103.8% (the two endpoints lie in different firmware phases). Hand-written from scratch on 09-17; on A0-VR, r0 ran at 96.05 ms / 57.24 TF/s (1/12.6 of same-session ASM); after 20 op-evolve rounds on A0-VR, r20 reached 511.42 TF/s (10.75 ms, ~0.71–0.72× of ASM); on B0, r29 (= r19h + u2n) reached 656.5 TF/s, 80.4% of ASM. The same r29 code on the same blocked ruler was only 69.5% of ASM on A0-VR (09-28), but 83.6% on A0 after the firmware reflash (09-30): estimated from r29, about 14 percentage points of the headline progress come from the reflash (FlyDSL is more clock-sensitive than ASM), not from the code. The 09-30 hand campaign s1–s6, built around a 3-stage TDM LDS ring plus reading the next iteration's B operand back into registers ahead of time, brought bwd to 5.295–5.300 ms / ≈1037–1038 TF/s, 103.8% of ASM (5.497–5.500 ms) (A0 after the reflash, blocked ruler, randn, same process, bitwise deterministic). A 10-02 re-check with real data gave an s6/ASM time ratio of 0.972 on the blk ruler and 1.038 on the gb ruler at the training operating point. s6 still issues 7 GEMMs, doing ~39% more matrix math than ASM.
  5. FlyDSL fwd: still ~8% slower, and 35% slower at the training operating point. The A0-VR fwd job went from 935.04 TF/s at r0 to 1111.15 TF/s at r11 (+18.8%; 78.9% of the same-session beat of 1407.8 TF/s, 79.4% against the bar of 1398.67 TF/s). On B0, speculative softmax was 4.6% faster on randn but actually slower with real data at the training clock; r16 turns speculation off (i.e., r13ns) and is 12–23% faster with real data plus the training clock. The current fwd champion is r16 = r13ns: on A0 after the reflash, measured on 09-30 at 1.353 ms / 1626 TF/s vs ASM 1.257 ms, a time ratio of 1.076 (92.9% of ASM); with real data on 10-02, blk 1.081 and gb 1.349. fwd is now the main remaining gap.
  6. e2e: FlyDSL is now on par with ASM. A0, 10-02 (driver 7.1.0-2412954, 32 layers, nkfix, NKFIX_CHECK=1, three arms alternating in the same process, median training sclk ~1.50 GHz): fwd r16 + bwd s6 at 1,349.4 / 1,349.9 ms per step and 24,283 / 24,275 tok/s, 0.999 of ASM (1,350.6 ms, 24,262 tok/s); fwd r16 + bwd r29 at 1.027. Split with CUDA events (kineto traces are invalid on A0): attention bwd is ~4 ms per step faster than ASM (adjacent-pair difference 4.5 ms; difference between the two arms' medians 3.8–4.4 ms), fwd is ~9.2 ms slower, so attention as a whole costs ~4.3–5.2 ms more, yet the step is 0.7–2.0 ms faster; this ~6 ms discrepancy (step − FA is −6.3 ms in both processes) lies in the parts outside attention or in timing noise and has not been broken down. Versus r29, s6 saves ~53 ms of attention bwd per step and the step is 37–39 ms faster, again with ~14–16 ms not broken down. Before that, the final B0 09-28 r16 + r29 stood at 1.0323 / 1.0328 (20,091 vs 20,747 tok/s). The product branch (r16 + r29, flydsl 0.2.4) measured 1,361.9 ms/step and 24,061 tok/s in the 10-05 tutorial, but that was with NKFIX_CHECK=0, so it cannot be compared directly with the NKFIX_CHECK=1 ASM 1,350.6 ms above (a direct comparison would make it look only 0.8% slower): correcting by the ~+33 ms per step of NKFIX_CHECK=1 (measured on B0), the branch is ~3% slower than ASM (estimated, not measured), consistent with the 1.027 of the flyr29 arm, which is also r16 + r29.
  7. The top e2e lever is actually GEMM (hipBLASLt). The first problem was a misplaced library path: 402 files sat in a library/gfx1250/ subdirectory; after setting HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH, A0-VR at 32 layers went from 244 to 2,027 tok/s (8.3×). The second problem was that the NN (dgrad) and wgrad layouts lacked pure-bf16 tuned libraries and fell back to the GEMV tile MT32x16x32, at only 50–80 TF/s, with GEMM taking 94–96% of the step. nkfix (rewriting the operand layout of aten::mm with TorchDispatchMode so that it hits MT256x256x128) took A0-VR at 8 layers from 6,128 to 37,746 tok/s (6.16×) and the B0 32-layer ASM arm from 2,016–2,022 to 20,718 tok/s (10.3×). Only once GEMM was fixed did ASM/FlyDSL attention account for ~15–22% of the step, so that op-level improvements became visible in e2e. The cost: 21% of the nkfix runs on A0 produced NaN (8/39 vs 0/35, p=0.004), so numbers such as 49,878, 11,608/5.85×, 38,043/6.21× and +14.40% were retracted; the B0 rewrite ran 8 times, 486 steps, with 0 NaNs. TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT is silently overridden in at least three places; the host hipBLASLt libraries are associated with several NaN and wedge incidents, so since 10-02 (h85) only the image's libraries are used.
  8. Wedges and AC power cycles are the largest time cost. Over 09-11..10-02, A0 went through 27 manual power cycles or reboots because of wedges ((d) d.1.4, deduplicated across sources; 25 if the two on 09-11 were warm reboots; 28 counting the ineffective AC cycle during the 09-29 driver/firmware mismatch), 9 of them on 09-16 alone; 82% of the wall-clock time of the five rounds on 09-21..22 went to wedges and waiting for AC. By the first dmesg line they fall into three classes: class A is the on-card fp32 Tensile reference GEMM going out of bounds, class B starts with an INVALIDATE_TLBS timeout, and class C is a fault storm set off by an out-of-bounds read in a candidate kernel. All four precursor discriminants proposed over time were falsified; the conclusion is that there is no usable predictor, so alerts are raised only on the wedge itself. The triggers actually located and eliminated: the on-card fp32 reference GEMM (refcache, h50), running several shapes in one process (now one shape per process), autotune inside training steps (now an offline table), inductor autotune caused by converters: [], PC sampling (permanently disabled), new kernels going straight to prod, and the host hipBLASLt libraries (h85: toy first, then prod; compile-only for reading VGPRs; image libraries only). Also, risk is priced per startup (startup-phase failures on 09-16: 3/14, i.e., 21%). Power cuts also truncated git objects twice (6 and 255 objects), which is where the rule "push at the end of every round" comes from.
  9. The rulers and criteria themselves rewrote the conclusions several times. Per-call interleaved timing systematically biased the FlyDSL/ASM ratio low (fwd by ~25%, bwd by ~3.4%); after switching to blocked timing, two false wins were retracted; conclusions can be opposite between randn and real data, and between the op clock and the training operating point (~1.28 GHz after GEMM bursts); the framework's criteria went wrong three times (A0 r15 promoted on an arithmetic mean, B0 r27 misjudged as target_met by geomean, A0 r24 a false positive from the fast median), each corrected by hand. After A0's 09-29 firmware reflash (VBIOS 630A→700E, SMU 125.7.1→125.12.0, with the driver changed at the same time from 7.1.1-2397345 to 7.1.0-2411946), the same code ran 1.24–1.67× faster at op level, and all A0 absolute numbers from before 09-29 are void. The e2e 1.44× (1,947→1,351 ms) cited by the tutorial is not purely the effect of the reflash: the 1,947 ms comes from the 09-28 batch of runs that was later voided as a whole (a0_p3a); when that process first loaded the FlyDSL fwd tree at step 2, the tree's _env.py repointed HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH to the host library (whether the subsequent GEMMs were affected is unproven); the 1,351 ms was measured on 10-02, and in between the driver was changed again on 10-01, to 7.1.0-2412954.
  10. Unfinished work and next steps. ① fwd: still ~8% slower at op level, 1.349× at the training operating point, ~9 ms per step in e2e. ② s6 has not yet been ported into the product branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt (the branch has r29, 1.235× of ASM at op level); the PR has not been opened yet, and the 1.032 cited in PR_BODY.md is an old cross-machine number from B0 09-28 that needs updating. ③ The gb scoring ruler for the training operating point is designed but not installed; the fwd job has been restored from the B0 backup but not launched; both need user approval. ④ The 5-GEMM fused version w4f is limited by whole-chip atomic throughput and is paused. ⑤ Unexplained phenomena: in e2e, ASM is faster than either op ruler measured it (suspected: the inputs are hot in MALL; not measured); the causes of class-B wedges and of startup-phase MES faults; the root cause of the nkfix NaNs on A0. Several skill documents also need correcting; see the end of section (d).

(a) Before optimization: what goes wrong when running the main branch as-is

Scope: the start-up phase, 2026-09-10..09-14. In this section "main" means the head of upstream main at the time, c1325c7e (#481, 2026-09-09, the first time a Triton dense attention backend was added for gfx1250). The first working branch, gfx1250-attn-dispatch-and-tuning, was branched off it (git merge-base f8c45dee origin/main = c1325c7e). The "current state" column in the tables has been checked against the local origin/main = 8cda13c7 (09-30). The target op is Llama-3.1-8B attention: b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16 causal (bottom-right), BSHD, GQA G=4.

Notation and conventions:

  • A0-VR: A0 (heliosr-1b114-c07-1) in its VR-throttled state before the 09-29 firmware reflash; sclk capped at 1100 MHz, ~0.97-1.07 GHz under load. A0-RF: after the 09-29 firmware reflash; ~1.74-2.03 GHz at op level, ~1.5 GHz during training. B0: ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1, 2133-2244 MHz under load. Absolute numbers from the three states are not comparable with one another, and the various "ratios" cannot stand in for one another either: ① Cross-machine A0-VR→B0: 5 like-for-like rows of the same Triton-family code give 1.88-2.01x (median 1.93x; all 7 rows of FINAL-TABLE give 1.88-2.12x, median 1.97x). This is a ratio between two cards, and B0's own dmesg also shows VR throttle warnings (the 0914 repro estimates their cost at ~9%), so it is not A0's own throttle cost (0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:32-38, 124-130). ② For ASM attention, also across machines, only the fwd ratio of 1.118x holds (A0-VR 09-15 1.572 ms vs B0 09-14 quiet window 1.406 ms, both ASM fwd on the tune ruler). The claim in 0915__opt/JIRA-TRACE-ANALYSIS.md:148-156 that "attention differs by only 1.12-1.15x across machines" (bwd 1.150x; total 11.726 vs 10.236 ms, 1.146x) does not hold: the bwd item compares A0's shim artifact at 10.160 ms (retracted 09-24) with B0's fused Triton bwd at 8.835 ms (on 09-14 B0 had no ASM bwd yet; --impl asm meant ASM forward + fused backward), which are not the same kernel. With fused Triton bwd on both sides, bwd is 17.735/8.835 ≈ 2.01x and the total is 19.285/10.235 = 1.88x (0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:17; 0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:102-103; 0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md:246; (d) d.3). ③ A0 itself, before→after the firmware reflash: 1.24-1.67x at op level with the same code and the same method (mean of the two orders). The e2e 1.44x is not a clean same-method comparison and is not purely a platform difference: the before value comes from the voided 09-28 run, a process in which the FlyDSL fwd tree repointed HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH to the host library midway; between the two runs the driver and the e2e suite both changed as well. It serves only as a reference (see the notes after the a.1 table).
  • FLOP convention: fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12, fwd+bwd 7.697e12 / call. The documents of the time reported only the fwd+bwd total, and that total is exactly the sum of the two. The "per-direction TF/s (converted)" column in the tables was computed in this section with the current convention.
  • Rulers: tune = tools/gfx1250/tune_attention.py (median of CUDA events, 20 iters / 5 warmup, 256 MiB L2 flush per rep, SQNR against an fp32 reference computed separately for each of the four tensors out/dq/dk/dv). bench0910 = the 09-10 log of the Primus-side attention bench (Primus:output/0910__fa_opt/attn_bench_gfx1250.log). E2 = E2_bench.py from op-evolve phase-1 (warmup 5, iters 20, repeated 3 times). bat = benchmark/ops/training/bench_attention_turbo.py (09-30).
  • Source paths: relative paths without a prefix are all under Primus-Turbo/output/. 0913/ = 0913__opt_plan__claude/; ph1/ = op-evolve:output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/ (op-evolve = /home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve/); Primus: = /home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0828__primus/Primus/; wt-llama31 README = wt-llama31/docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/README.md (the 10-05 tutorial).

Clock (the most important variable)VR throttle. Every amdgpu init prints WARN: GPU is throttled, expect performance decrease. VR.. The sclk DPM has only two levels left, 500 / 1100 MHz; MAX_CLK = 1100. On 09-04 the same card could still run 1699-1703 MHz under load (cap 2400). The throttle first appears at the 09-10 22:38 boot, and later reboots did not clear it. Measured under load: attention 1067 MHz / 1227 W (only 49% of the power used), Triton GEMM 1008 MHz / 1770 W, which shows the card is pinned by its clock rather than held back by the power limitThe "throttle cost" has three conventions; they do not measure the same thing and cannot stand in for one another: ① on 09-13, estimated from the clock ratio (1699-1703 MHz on 09-04 vs the 1100 MHz cap) at ~1.65x (the original gives no formula; computing from these two numbers gives ~1.55x); ② the 09-14 document obtained 1.88-2.01x (median 1.93x) from a 5-row same-code comparison on B0 and wrote it as "1.65x → ~1.95x", but that is the A0-VR→B0 cross-card ratio (B0 itself also has VR warnings, at a cost of ~9%), not A0's own throttle cost; ③ same-code comparison on A0 itself, before→after the firmware reflash: 1.24-1.67x at op level (mean of the two orders); the e2e 1.44x is not purely a platform difference, as it also mixes in differences in the BLAS library environment and in the e2e suite, and the driver was changed one more time (see the table below and the notes after it). Lifted on 09-29 after the admin reflashed the VBIOS (630A→700E) and SMU (125.7.1→125.12.0): the VR warning is still printed at boot, but the DPM table is no longer truncated at 1100 MHz. StabilityFrom 09-04 to 09-13 the card wedged 5 times in total, all with the same signature: MES failed to respond → driver reset stuck at wait for reset ack → D-state tasks pile up → only a host reboot helps; reloading the driver does not. The first two were unrelated to this project: 09-04 was HipKittens GEMM; 09-06/07 was a grouped-GEMM job, with SMU: No response 857 times. The two on 09-11 were triggered by us: E3 evaluated the full 8192² mask_mod on the GPU → GPU Hang; E4 used rocprofv3 PC sampling → memory fault → MES wedged for good. The 5th was on 09-13 at about 13:30 UTC; a 4-worker queue was running at the time and in-flight kernels were repeatedly interrupted with pkill -9, which is suspected to be the trigger. After the card wedges, rocm-smi, ps … wchan, pgrep and docker exec all hang; only dmesg with a timeout still worksOver the whole campaign (09-11..10-02), A0 needed 27 manual power cycles or reboots in total because of wedges. Counting the ineffective AC cycle on 09-29 during the driver/firmware mismatch, it is 28; if the two on 09-11 were warm reboots, it is 25. For the per-day distribution see (d) d.1.4ph1/HARDWARE-ISSUE.md §2.1; 0913/phase2/INCIDENT-2026-09-13-wedge.md; 0913/PROGRESS.md

BLASIn the amdprimus image every matmul throws HIPBLAS_STATUS_INVALID_VALUE (Cannot read …/library/TensileLibrary_lazy_gfx1250.dat), and the only option is to set TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=0 and fall back to rocBLAS. In the extended-v2 image hipBLASLt runs once HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH is set, but every layout gets only 60-92 TF/s. On 09-11 E1 measured Triton (inductor) bf16 GEMM at 1002.7 TF/s @1008 MHz (best of 4 shapes), which was then taken as this card's compute roof. HBM measured 6.46-6.48 TB/s (best of 4 PyTorch elementwise patterns), only 28% of the amd-smi nominal 23,347 GB/s; the cause is unresolved: the VR throttle, the test not being a tuned STREAM, and the nominal value being unreachable in itself cannot be told apartThe root cause was rewritten twice over time, see a.3 #3. 1002.7 was later shown not to be the roof: on 09-15, on the same card at the same clock (A0-VR), hipBLASLt's NT path measured 1502 TF/s at 8192³, and after nkfix changed the layout the best single shape reached 1628 (dgrad mlp) / 1742 TF/s (lm_head, including transpose overhead)ph1/E1.md §5 (:200-222); ph1/HARDWARE-ISSUE.md S2; 0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §5, §11; 0915__opt/GEMM-NN-FINDING.md:70, 84-86, 118
ProfilingIn the container, the rocprofv3 runtime of ROCm 7.14 exposes only 51 counters. Of the 27 requested, only 8 were available, with no counters in the byte, matrix-unit, stall or occupancy categories. When unknown counters are mixed in, it exits 0 but the corresponding columns are silently dropped. Every PC sampling attempt triggered a GPU fault (E4 recorded 2, the 09-13 document records 3); one of them caused a wedge, after which it was disabled across the boardATT became usable after the 09-29 firmware reflashph1/E4.md Headline, §6; 0913/phase0/PLATFORM-ESCALATION.md

Same-code comparison on A0 itself before and after the firmware reflash (A0's own throttle cost. For the op rows the commands, arm directories and blocked ruler are all unchanged and only the platform changed: the firmware, together with the driver swapped during the same period; the two cannot be separated (REPORT §6.3). The e2e row is an exception, see the notes after the table. This is late-stage optimization code, listed here only to give this ratio):

e2e per step, ASM attention + nkfix (NKFIX_CHECK=1)1,947 ms (09-28 a0_p3a, from the voided batch of runs, see below)1,351 ms (1350.6; 10-02, new e2e suite, median sclk during training ~1.50 GHz)1.44x (not purely a platform difference, see below)0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §2, §5.3, §6.4; 1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md, E2E-PLAN.md §1-§3; README §7

The clock ratio under load is ~1.75x, and every measured speedup is below it: FlyDSL is more sensitive to sclk, while ASM fwd barely changes with sclk. In the same section the other FlyDSL fwd arms give 1.45-1.69x per order (0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.1, §6.3). The FlyDSL fwd speedup is 1.47-1.50x depending on the order: the 1.48x in the REPORT §6.3 summary is the mean of the two orders, while the 1.50x in wt-llama31 README §7 takes only o1.

The e2e row is not a clean same-code, same-method comparison; the 1.44x is for reference only:

  • The before value of 1,947 ms is the per-step median of the asm arm in 09-28 a0_p3a, from that single process only (the reverse-order a0_p3b produced NaN and was voided). REPORT §6.4 has voided the 09-28 A0 absolute numbers wholesale, and E2E-PLAN.md §1 also records them as "void numbers, keep only the lessons". This process called FlyDSL fwd for the first time at step 2, and the fwd tree's _env.py:26 repointed HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH to the host library ~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250 at import; whether the later asm steps were affected was not verified (REPORT §5.3).
  • The after value comes from a separately written suite of 10-02, 1002__e2e/e2e/: fwd uses fwd_r16_imglib, a copy with those two repointing lines deleted, the adapter layer adds a BLAS guard, and steps 1-2 run ASM only (E2E-PLAN.md §2-§3).
  • Between the two runs, the driver was first changed on 09-28/29 via -2410994 and -2411826 to 7.1.0-2411946 (the one in place for the 09-30 op re-measurement), and then on 10-01 again to 7.1.0-2412954, one change more than for the op rows.
  • The only things the two sides have in common are: nkfix (0927__b0/gemm/nkfix_b0.py, unchanged in the git history since 09-28 05:12) plus NKFIX_CHECK=1, the same 32L config template (0927__b0/e2e/configs/l8b_e2e.template.yaml), and the practice of alternating 3 arms step by step over ~90 steps. wt-llama31 README §7 also counts this row as "Same code and method", which is inaccurate.

1Under the image's torch 2.11, main crashes as soon as it is importedimport primus_turbo.pytorch raises TypeError: Opaque type ... must subclass torch._opaque_base.OpaqueBase. 2Default dispatch cannot pick the only backend that runs on gfx1250When no backend is pinned, dispatch for flash_attn_func (BSHD b4) goes as follows: FLYDSL is tried first and rejects G=4; then _DENSE_FWD_BACKENDS is scanned in insertion order (FLYDSL→AITER→HIPKITTENS→GLUON→TRITON), and AITER matches first. The amdprimus image has no aiter, so the very first training step fails with ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'aiter' → ImportError: Primus-Turbo requires amd-aiter==0.1.14.post1. If aiter is installed, fwd passes, but bwd fails midway through a training step with invalid argument for fmha_bwdDenseAttnFwdAiterBackend.can_handle returns True for any 4-D fp16/bf16 input without looking at the architecture. The AITER path ultimately lands in CK, and CK's fmha only has CDNA kernelsThe only backend that runs on gfx1250 is the Triton backend added by c1325c7e, whose can_handle accepts only is_gfx1250, but default dispatch never reaches it; the only option is to set PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_BACKEND=TRITON by hand. Moreover, the error surfaces midway through training, not at dispatch timeOn the branch, f8c45dee (09-13) makes AITER dense refuse calls on gfx1250. The 09-30 port branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt makes FLYDSL resolve to the new kernels on gfx1250 (G∈{1,2,4,8,16}, d128, bf16). origin/main 8cda13c7 has still not fixed it0913/phase0/PHASE0-STATUS.md:11-22; git show c1325c7e (commit message; attention_impl.py:122-127, 431-436, 621-626); session 541e7bc3 (aiter ImportError); 0930__port/PR_BODY.md

After upstream 9f05cd85 (#509, 09-17) relaxed the GQA gate to powers of 2 in [1,256], the two gates that had been covering for each other opened on main at the same time: Llama calls with SBHD or b=1 BSHD enter the gfx950 JIT and fail with requires gfx950+ (uses ds_read_tr16_b64). 8The Triton backend's factory-default config is slow, ~1.9x slower than the incumbent flexOn A0-VR, bench0910 (the 0.4.1.dev12 that ships with the image) measured fwd 10.366 / bwd 48.059 / total 58.424 ms = 131.7 TF/s, vs flex at 31.508 ms: 1.85x slower. The 09-13 tune bake-off was measured on the branch tree (the factory-default config as of @1cb2e183, not main as-is): 10.673 / 48.969 / 59.642 ms = 129.0 TF/s, flex 31.337 ms, 1.90x slower. These two code states must not be conflated: on B0 with the same ruler, @1cb2e183 factory default gives 28.121 ms (bwd 22.942) and @c1325c7e as-is gives 30.258 ms (bwd 25.025), a 7.6% difference (FINAL-TABLE footnote †). Per kernel (bench0910): _bwd_kernel_dkdv 35.92 ms (61.5%), _bwd_kernel_dq 11.75 ms (20.1%), attn_fwd 9.94 ms (17.0%), preprocess 0.06 ms. On B0 (09-14, tune, main c1325c7e as-is): 30.258 ms = 254.4 TF/s, flex 15.307 ms, 1.98x slowerThe @triton.autotune of both fwd and bwd has only 1 config (num_stages=1, num_warps=4), _bwd_preprocess_use_o does not even have the decorator, and FIXED_BLOCK_M = FIXED_BLOCK_N = 64 are module constants. The whole parameter set was copied verbatim from a perf-kernel targeting CDNA (wave64, MFMA, 64 KB LDS) and was never searched on gfx1250 (wave32, WMMA, 320 KB LDS); autotune effectively serves only as a compile cache. In addition, dense_backward allocates dq/dk/dv in fp32, and autograd then casts them back to bf16 (bfloat16_copy ×3, 0.182 ms in total; Fill ×5, 0.144 ms in total). This is memory and cast overhead, not a precision bugAt the op level, turning on turbo attention is actually a regression; the dkdv kernel alone accounts for 61.5%Changing just two integers (fwd num_stages 1→2, bwd num_warps 4→2) brought the branch tree on A0-VR to 35.704-36.405 ms, 1.64-1.66x faster, with SQNR unchanged and 1000 runs bitwise identical. After that came the vendored aiter fused backward (1cb2e183), the ASM fwd (0e5cb743) and so on, see (c). The Triton kernel on origin/main has not been touched up to 8cda13c7: on 09-30 on A0-RF, bat measured fwd 5.11 ms / 430.45 TF/s and bwd 24.49 ms / 224.50 TF/s. Same day, same ruler, the geometric-mean speedup of the port branch's FlyDSL over this Triton backend on 36 in-gate shapes: causal fwd 4.13x / bwd 3.37x, non-causal fwd 3.47x / bwd 3.58x (A0-RF, 18 shapes each)bench0910; 0913/phase1/BAKEOFF.md §1; 0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §3, §12; PHASE0-STATUS.md:54-67 and the "Corrections" section; git show c1325c7e:primus_turbo/triton/attention/attention_kernel.py:452-480, 1095-1118; 0914__repro__c07/FINAL-TABLE.md; 0930__port/runs/bench_turbo_triton.csv:55; 0930__port/PR_BODY.md "Performance" (recomputed from runs/bench_turbo_{flydsl,triton}.csv)
9The backward autotune key is wrong (latent bug)The bwd key list contains BLOCK_DMODEL, but neither of the two backward kernels has that parameter; at the same time HQ/HK and seqlen are missing from itTriton silently drops unknown keys, and it only computes the key when len(configs)>1Harmless while there is only one config; as soon as there are several configs, tuning results get reused under the wrong keyFixed on the branch by df20f1d6 (09-13); unchanged on origin/main (this kernel file has not been touched since c1325c7e)0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §9; git show c1325c7e:primus_turbo/triton/attention/attention_kernel.py:1095-1110
10The Triton two-kernel backward has a "silently wrong results" trapFIXED_BLOCK_M is in fact a cross-kernel LSE/delta ABI: attn_fwd writes LSE at m*BLOCK_M*2, preprocess writes delta at +BLOCK_M, the host side rebuilds them via _lse_delta_views, and dkdv splits them apart with 2*start_m+arange(0,2*BLOCK_M) plus two tl.gather calls. Changing only dkdv's BLOCK_M raises no error, but dk/dv become smoothly wrong. sequence_parallel=False sets grid dim1 to 1, so only 1/128 of the gradient is computed and the rest keeps its initial zerosThe ABI is implicit, without a single assertionA sweep that looks only at timing will take a wrong config for a big win. This structure also cannot express asymmetric tiles like aiter's (dkdv 32×128, dq 128×32), which is exactly the structural reason it lost the 09-13 bake-off to aiterThis ABI was left alone; instead aiter's single-kernel fused backward was vendored (1cb2e183); tune gained a four-tensor SQNR gate0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §8, §13; 0913/phase1/BAKEOFF.md §3
11The repo's own measurement and validation cannot be trustedThe existing bench's correctness check returns the same max-abs value 8.011e-03 for every backend, and it does not look at gradients. bench_attention.py --mask block_causal does not actually build a document mask and is identical to causal (31.008 vs 31.012 ms). The commit message of c1325c7e claims 220.6 TF/s (b2 s8192, SDPA flash at 161.4), whereas the same kernel measured on A0-VR gives only b2 129.42 / b4 127.83 TF/smax-abs only looks at out; the mask argument only changes the batch dim of the BlockMask. The measurement conditions behind 220.6 are unknown: the commit message only says "seq 8192, B2 H32 D128, fwd+bwd", without hkv, whether it is causal, or the clock. 220.6/129.4 = 1.70x; on 09-13 this was compared against the 1.65x clock ratio estimated at the time, and 220.6 was judged to have been measured on an unthrottled card. This is only an inference: 220.6 is far above what A0-VR measures, so the direction is probably right (main as-is with b4 on B0 gives 254.4), but on which card and at which clock it was measured is still unknown. The 1.65x is only a clock estimate; A0-VR→B0 is a cross-card ratio between two cards (median over 5 rows 1.93x), while A0's own op-level ratio before vs after the reflash is 1.24-1.67x; there is no conclusion as to which of these ratios the 1.70x matchesIt cannot catch wrong gradients, and it makes it easy to compare numbers taken at different clocks side by sideWrote our own tune: SQNR computed separately for each of the four tensors (threshold 50 dB), the config actually used is asserted at launch, rc=139 is recorded as a retry, and the health check only reads dmesgph1/E2.md §4; ph1/E3.md Headline; 0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §2; git show -s c1325c7e; PHASE0-STATUS.md "Tooling"; 0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:124-130; wt-llama31 README §7
12The alternative backend, aiter Triton MHA, was never tuned on gfx1250 and also has numerical trapsgfx1250-MHA-DEFAULT.json has the same md5 as the gfx950 version (dd06bae7…), and it even contains the CDNA-only knob matrix_instr_nonkdim; fwd uses num_stages=1. Factory-default performance: 34.111 ms (E2, 09-11, extended-v2 + amd-aiter 0.1.14.post1, 225.6 TF/s); 34.565 ms (tune, 09-13, aiter-src @ffa945f9, 222.7 TF/s). mha_fused_bwd is numerically broken (dk −0.22 dB, dq 46.61, dv 49.03), and slower too (42.294 ms). When BLOCK_N1=256 is not paired with BLOCK_M2, dq is only 9.59 dB, yet it is 1.31x faster. BLOCK_M2=64,BLOCK_N2=128 raises CompilationError. There are also 7x performance cliffs, e.g. BLK_SLICE_FACTOR=4 151.6 ms, waves_per_eu=4 120.6 ms. _get_config carries @functools.lru_cache, so unless cache_clear() is called first, overrides silently have no effect; a whole sweep round was voided because of thisThe config was copied over from gfx950 and never searched on gfx1250. In the single-kernel backward, the dkdv half and the dq half share the same launch grid, so BLOCK_N1 is coupled with BLOCK_M2It is not on the default path of turbo main (aiter must be installed separately). But it won the 09-13 bake-off, and the in-tree fused backward was later derived from itThe 09-11 "aiter + 2 knobs = 24.347 ms / 316.1 TF/s" is retracted: re-measured on 09-13 under the same convention it is 31.207 ms / 246.6 TF/s. The reason is that upstream aiter changed the backward factory-default config, so bwd num_warps 4→2 no longer has any effect. The upper bound of pure-aiter tuning is 21.672 ms / 355.1 TF/s (N1=M2=256, BSF=1; DECISIONS D9). PROGRESS.md and the 0914 FINAL-TABLE cite 21.684 / 354.9; 5 repeats of the same config in phase2/ledgers/wq.jsonl (tagged rep 1-5) give 21.527-21.713 ms, so both numbers fall within that range, and they differ from each other by 0.06%. 0915__repro__c07 judged the 09-13 34.565 "untrustworthy" on the grounds that A0/B0 = 1.63x, but that 1.63x was computed from the 28.035 newly measured on 09-15. The 09-13 value is 2.01x B0's 17.204, consistent with the 1.88-2.12x of the other FINAL-TABLE rows, and 34.111 was measured independently on 09-11 on a different image. So the run more likely to be anomalous is the 09-15 one (its fwd of 3.725 ms looks like a tuned forward). 13aiter's precompiled ASM kernels for gfx1250 are "there but out of reach", and the backward writes out of bounds under GQA (found 09-14..09-17; this is the upstream status quo)Only 52 .co files are published for gfx1250 (gfx950 has 1466, per the VENDOR-REPORT count; counting gfx950 with find in the local aiter-src @6963ae9 gives 1459, source of the discrepancy not investigated). The backward has only 6 .co files (gfx950 has 124): bwd_hd128_bf16_{a32_pssk,a32_pssk_perf,causal_br_a32_pssk,causal_br_a32_pssk_perf} plus bwd_hd128_dq_convert_bf16 and bwd_hd128_odo_bf16. The file names in the same gfx950 directory include psskddv (52), a16 (26), fp16 (45), swa (4) and group (50) variants; gfx1250 has none of them. There is no varlen backward either (the dispatch table only has mode=0). On the aiter Python side, can_impl_fmha_v3_bwd checks starting from gfx942 and only widens to gfx950, so gfx1250 can never be selected. The grid of the main backward kernel is (kv_tiles, nhead_q, batch), yet it indexes the kv-sized dk/dv by q head: at ratio=4, dq 52.24 / dk −0.94 / dv −0.65 dB (A0-VR). At s=1024 this is silent corruption; at s=256 the process faults, but the card is not harmedUpstream assets and wrappers were only completed for CDNA. Under GQA, ratio workgroups write the same dk/dv tile without synchronizationaiter's fastest kernels on gfx1250 cannot be called directly; the only way is to reverse-engineer the kernarg offline from the ELF metadata and write our own launcher. The workaround is to allocate dk/dv per q head and then reduce on the host side, at a cost of 1.254 GiB extra at peak plus 0.482 ms for the reduction (A0-VR, +5.2% backward time)0e5cb743 (09-14) hooked up the ASM fwd. The ASM bwd was hooked up on 09-15: a hand-written launcher of about 674 lines, using the workaround above, with an eligibility gate of seqlen>=2048. On 09-17 a vendor report to AITER was written. For performance and the remaining issues see (b)0917__flydsl/VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md §1-§2; /home/lihuzhan/code/aiter-src/hsa/{gfx1250,gfx950}/fmha_v3_bwd/*.co (@6963ae9 file listing); 0917__flydsl/GQA-WORKAROUND-COST.md; 0915__opt/BACKEND-STRATEGY.md:57,73; 0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md §1.1

16The Triton backward once produced dk/dv = −inf (unexplained)On 09-13 on A0-VR, it happened once with config fwd:num_stages=2; bwd:num_warps=2,waves_per_eu=1: out/dq normal, dk/dv = −inf dB (about 1/100 of runs). It has not reproduced in the 2,300 calls since (1000+1000 in-process, plus 150 fresh processes with 2 calls each), and both the factory-default and the tuned configs were bitwise consistent. An earlier signal that "the factory-default config's dk is nondeterministic" was actually caused by the fp32 reference itself being nondeterministic (torch.matmul goes through rocBLAS); this has been correctedUnknown. The candidate explanation is a transient hardware fault already on record for this cardAn occasional wrong gradient that slips past a single check is exactly the kind of error most likely to get shippedKept the four-tensor gate. The determinism check now compares bitwise against itself, rather than comparing SQNR against a reference recomputed every time0913/phase1/RESULTS.md §4, §12

2turbo attention never actually entered e2e (three linked problems: patch, enable_gqa, converter)① Under the default config, the turbo_attention patch was in fact applied (12/12), and the run then died at the first step with No module named 'aiter'. With the converter off, inner_attention remains torchtitan's FlexAttentionWrapper. And TurboAttention.forward does not accept enable_gqaIn the 09-13 "turbo:TRITON tuned 245 tps vs flex 244 tps", both sides were actually flex: that day's repro_l8b_bf16_mbs4_seq8k_turbo.yaml (and _turbo_ac.yaml) itself contains converters: []. The Primus version and config files used on 09-14 B0 are not on this machine and cannot be checked, so the real source of the 1.459x is unresolved; the only certainty is that it does not come from attention. On the same day, the ASM forward was never called in B0 training either: after the gate was made to write its own trace file, the file was never created during 21 training steps, i.e. asm_forward_eligible was never reached at all; the criterion "no aiter banner in the log" that the three earlier rounds of diagnosis relied on was itself broken, because AITER_LOG_LEVEL=ERROR suppressed the banner. The three conditions "converter on, compile on, fused backward running" had never all held at the same time before thisOnce the converter was turned on (09-14), 6 layers of problems were hit and fixed one after another. 3e2e choked by GEMM/BLASOnce it ran through on A0-VR on 09-13: 245 tps, 133.7 s/step, 14.17 TFLOPS, GPU 100% busy the whole time. The entire attention path is 35.7 ms × 32 = 1.14 s, under 1% of the step. In the amdprimus image, hipBLASLt fails with HIPBLAS_STATUS_INVALID_VALUE as soon as it is called, so the only option was TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=0, falling back to rocBLAS. rocBLAS on an idle card: bf16 8192³ 40.12 ms = 27.4 TF/s; qkv 32768×4096×6144 24.0, mlp up 23.9, mlp down 26.1 TF/s. This attribution was later rewritten twice, see the right-hand columnNo e2e number from before the optimization can either verify or falsify an attention change. JIRA's MI455X numbers (GEMM 655 ms/step, 19,795 tok/s) cannot have come from A0 with this imageThe first rewrite came on 09-14: on B0 (container fa-e2e) the library was present and hipBLASLt worked, but at only 112.98 TF/s, while a naive Triton GEMM reached 1190.07 TF/s, a 10.5x gap. The second rewrite came on 09-15: the problem with the A0 image turned out to be a path mismatch: the 402 files sit in the library/gfx1250/ subdirectory, but the loader looks one level up. With HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH=…/_rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250 set, 8192³ NN reaches 68.74 TF/s, and 32L e2e goes 244→2,027 tps (8.3x, 3 steps, n=1, A0-VR). This fix has two environment traps: the library copy under _rocm_sdk_devel/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250 is incomplete and pointing at it gives a core dump / SIGSEGV, so only the _rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250 copy can be used; a bash -lc login shell reads a profile script that forces TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=0 (our container has one), so use bash -c, set both variables explicitly, and then check torch.backends.cuda.preferred_blas_library() inside the process. The same day the real big-ticket item was found: hipBLASLt ships a pure-bf16 tuned library only for the forward's TN layout (BB_BB_UA_Type_BB_HPA); dgrad (NN, Ailk_Bljk) and wgrad go through a GridBased nearest-neighbor table lookup and land on the GEMV tile MT32x16x32, reaching only 50-80 TF/s, 16.5-21.5x slower than TN at the same shape; whereas, on the same card at the same clock, the torch-level NT path (a @ bt.T / F.linear, which lands on hipBLASLt's TN tuned library) measured 1502 TF/s at 8192³, faster even than the Triton 1002.7 treated as the roof on 09-11. The e2e cost while GEMM-NN was unfixed: on B0 on 09-28 (hipBLASLt path fixed, no nkfix), whichever attention was used, 32L reached only 1,856-1,922 tok/s (low-tier median; high tier 2,008-2,022), 17,049-17,655 ms/step, of which the backward MT32x16x32 GEMMs took 15.3-16.1 s/step. Fixes: on B0 on 09-14, compile plus forced inductor Triton GEMM, 4.01x faster; on 09-15/16, nkfix (rewriting the operand layout of aten::mm with TorchDispatchMode); on 09-28, nkfix_b0 + transpose_triton ported to B0, with tps about 10 times the pre-fix level. 6torch.compile: off because it can wedge the card, and turning it on by itself is a net lossThe repro config has compile.enable: false, with a comment saying that on this machine inductor's triton autotune of TransformerBlock triggers hipErrorLaunchFailure and kills the GPU. FlexAttentionWrapper._compiled_flex_attn is a ClassVar that is compiled at class definition time, so compile.enable: false has no control over it. B0 09-14: compile alone, 2,394→2,295 tps (−4%), peak memory 380.1→260.7 GiBBy default inductor leaves matmuls on aten.mm (i.e. hipBLASLt) and only fuses elementwise opscompile alone cannot solve the GEMM problem. 7Measurement and semantic traps of torchtitan out of the box (discovered only later)① When get_peak_flops() meets a device_name it does not recognize (this machine reports "AMD Radeon Graphics"), it falls back to the A100's 312 TF/s, so the entire MFU column in the logs is invalid: 09-13's 4.54% = 14.17/312, B0's 44.44% = 138.64/312, and later even 178%, 215.63% and 245% appeared. ② On a single card with debug.seed set to None, set_determinism simply returns without seeding: 5 runs gave 5 different step-1 losses, and the two A/B arms were also initialized differently. ③ 8B_flex calls a torch.compile'd create_block_mask once every step to build a block_causal mask, but Primus llama3's Attention.forward simply drops the mask when calling inner_attention(xq,xk,xv), so what is actually computed is plain causaltorchtitan only recognizes NVIDIA device names; the single-card branch returns early; the Primus and torchtitan interfaces are inconsistentMFU cannot be quoted; only tps and tflops can be used. Unseeded A/Bs mix in initialization noise. What is computed is not the same computation as JIRA's flex (block_causal): E3 measured the block_causal density at 99.25%, so there is no performance difference, but losses cannot be compared directlyReport only tps/tflops, using a measured peak as the denominator when needed (the 1002.7 of 09-11 is not the roof, see the BLAS row of a.1); debug.seed: 1234 fixed from 09-15 on; the mask problem is still unfixed to this day0915__opt/GEMM-WGRAD-FINDING.md:78-94; 0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:189-201; 0927__b0/e2e/VERIFY.md §1-§3; ph1/E3.md Headline

Notes:

  • The e2e of "main + PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_BACKEND=TRITON" has never been measured. The 10-05 tutorial estimated it from op-level times and in-training attention times at ~1.9-2.05 s/step (~16-17k tok/s). This estimate presupposes that nkfix and the other GEMM fixes are already in place, and it is on A0-RF, so it is not the "pre-optimization" state (wt-llama31 README §2, §7).
  • The 32L e2e baseline of 1,984 tok/s used by the later ladder comes from A0-VR on 09-15, when the hipBLASLt path had already been corrected and attention was already ASM fwd + fused bwd, so it is not a main number. See (b)(c).
  • The 245 tok/s of A0 on 09-13 and the 2,394 tok/s of B0 on 09-14 cannot be treated as a comparison under the same config: the clocks differ (A0-VR vs B0), and so do the BLAS paths (A0 uses rocBLAS, B0 uses hipBLASLt). Both machines run torchtitan v0.2.2 (see a.1). Both are choked by BLAS, with attention taking under 1% (A0) or only a small share (B0).
  • e2e baselines while the GEMM-NN problem was unfixed (hipBLASLt path fixed, no nkfix): 09-15 A0-VR 32L 2,027 tok/s (3 steps, n=1); 09-28 B0 32L 1,856-1,922 tok/s (17.0-17.7 s/step, attention already one of the three: Triton / ASM / FlyDSL), see a.3 #3.

Summary. Before optimization, running Llama-3.1-8B attention with main on gfx1250 had three layers of problems:

  1. By default it does not run at all. Under torch 2.11 it crashes on import; the default dispatch lands on CK, which supports only CDNA; all tests are skipped, so there is no signal whatsoever.
  2. The only backend that can be pinned by hand, Triton, uses a single config copied verbatim from CDNA and is ~1.9x slower than the incumbent flex, with dkdv accounting for 61.5%.
  3. e2e cannot detect any attention change. First, the image's BLAS was broken or extremely slow (rocBLAS 27.4 TF/s; later traced to a path mismatch, plus the missing tuned library for the NN layout). Second, converters: [] meant that what ran in e2e was always flex. Third, Primus's turbo default config switches on FP8/MX linear as well; in the 09-14 "turbo vs flex 1.459x", both arms were flex, and the real source of the difference is still unresolved.

In addition, all numbers on A0 were taken under the VR throttle (1100 MHz), and from 09-04..09-13 this card wedged on average about once every two days (5 times in total). So every absolute number in this section must carry a machine and clock label, and for cross-machine and cross-clock ratios it must also be made clear which kind they are (see "Notation and conventions").

(b) Performance of each backend after adaptation

Op shape: Llama-3.1-8B attention, b4 s8192 hq32 hkv8 d128 bf16, causal (Sq=Skv; bottom-right and top-left are equivalent), BSHD, GQA 4.

FLOP convention: all TF/s in the tables are computed with op-evolve tools/op_flops.py: fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12 (bwd uses the nominal 5-GEMM count). The source documents of 09-13..09-15 report only the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 / total time); per-direction TF/s marked * in the tables are converted from per-direction times. The in-tree vendored fused backward actually issues 7 GEMM passes, issued = 7.6975e12 = 1.400× nominal (7/5); our FlyDSL bwd has the same 7-GEMM structure plus diagonal-block granularity (×1.00546), issued = 7.7395e12 = 1.40764× nominal. This section never uses the issued convention (0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:121-122; 0923__flydsl/hint.md:2104-2110).

vs ASM = this row's time / the aiter ASM time in the same epoch; >1 means slower than ASM.

  • "same-process": measured alternating with ASM in the same process; the most trustworthy.
  • "≈": same machine and same clock state, but cross-session or cross-ruler; only the order of magnitude is meaningful.
  • Absolute values are not comparable across epochs; only ratios within the same epoch can be compared.

Source paths: relative to Primus-Turbo/output/ by default.

  • OE: = /home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve
  • skill: = Primus-Turbo/.claude/skills/gfx1250-attn-campaign/
  • WT: = worktree wt-llama31 (branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt)

Measurement epochs (the tables below are grouped by these):

Code Machine / clock state Date Main ruler
E1A0 heliosr-1b114-c07-1 single card, VR throttle (DPM cap 1100 MHz, ~943–1069 MHz under load)09-10..09-17tools/gfx1250/tune_attention.py: iters 20 / warmup 5, median of CUDA events, 256 MiB L2 flush per rep, SQNR ≥ 50 dB on four tensors; timing includes the autograd plumbing
E2B0 ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1, 4 cards loaded simultaneously, 2133–2244 MHz09-14same tune_attention.py as E1
E3A0 still throttled (prod timing window 989–1069 MHz)09-17..09-27op-evolve benchmark.py: times kernel launches only, median n=51, palindromic interleaving, 3 s warmup; fwdab.py ABBA
E3′A0's last measurement before the firmware reflash, still VR-throttled (op-level sclk 1020–1061 MHz); VBIOS 630A, SMU 125.7.1, driver amdgpu-dkms 7.1.1-239734509-28blocked randn ruler (prod: one process per order; A/A replicas in the same process); e2e: 3 arms alternating per step in the same process
E4B0 (2356 MHz idle; clocks not recorded per run at op level; 1.25–1.45 GHz during training)09-27..09-28per-call interleaving at first; from 09-28 switched to blocked timing (fwd ~03:40, bwd ~05:00 UTC, lead 4 + block 9, A/A ±0.2%); also opcheck.py
E5A0 after the 09-29 firmware reflash: VBIOS 700E, SMU 125.12.0, op level 1.74–2.03 GHz, ~1.5 GHz in training; driver 7.1.0-2411946 on 09-29, 7.1.0-2412954 from 10-0109-30..10-05blocked randn, same process; on 10-02 also two real-data rulers, blk / gb; bench_attention_turbo.py

A0 absolute numbers from E1/E3/E3′ are void after the 09-29 firmware reflash. After the reflash the clock rose by ~1.75×, but the speedup is not uniform across kernels (ASM fwd 1.24×, FlyDSL bwd 1.67×), so old data cannot be converted with a single factor (0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.3). With the same blocked ruler and the same set of arms, r13ns/ASM fell from 1.29 in E3′ to 1.08 in E5 and r29/ASM from 1.44 to 1.20: the ratios themselves also shift with the clock epoch. The firmware reflash and the driver change fell in the same period (7.1.1-2397345 → 7.1.0-2411946, then -2412954 on 10-01), so the effects cannot be strictly attributed to one or the other (ibid. §6.3). The same holds for e2e: the ASM arm going from 1,947 ms in E3′ to 1,350.6 ms in E5 (≈1.44×) is not purely a platform difference — besides the firmware, the driver changed twice between the two runs; moreover, in that E3′ process (a0_p3a), after the first FlyDSL call (step 2), HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH had already been repointed to the host library by the fwd tree's _env.py, and whether hipBLASLt rereads this variable mid-run has not been verified (0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §5.3).

b.1 What adaptations it took to get these backends running

Backend Key adaptations commit / path
Primus-Turbo Triton dense backend (main #481 c1325c7e, the only in-tree backend that runs on gfx1250)
  • Under torch 2.11, register_opaque_type in low_precision.py made the import fail; patched locally first
  • Default dispatch could not pick Triton: AITER registers first and its can_handle accepts any architecture, so dispatch landed on the CDNA-only CK and the backward failed with invalid argument. The fix keeps the AITER dense backend out of dispatch on gfx1250
  • The FlyDSL gate was written as >=(9,5), which gfx1250 also passes; changed to an exact gfx950 check; added a guard around flydsl's module-level import
  • conftest skipped everything on gfx1250; changed to an opt-out marker; runnable tests 0 → 36 → 51
  • The autotune list had only one config, copied from CDNA; made it tunable (PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_TRITON_TUNE): fwd num_stages=2, bwd num_warps=2
  • upstream f5e1f18b (#501)
  • f8c45dee, df20f1d6, 5eba2cf4, d00ba261
  • product branch c36cc124
In-tree vendored aiter onekernel fused Triton backward
  • Vendored aiter-src @ffa945f9 (MIT, ~1790 lines); gated on parallelism rather than sequence length
  • Fixed it being unusable under torch.compile
  • fwd num_warps=2 plus no waves_per_eu in bwd: total +4.28%
  • TRITON_HIP_USE_IN_THREAD_TRANSPOSE: backward −9%
1cb2e183, 03a76f61→8dd2fd32, ad67a2cc, 566e7798, a009599b
aiter Triton MHA (measurement only, not in-tree)
  • The amdprimus image has no aiter; git-cloned aiter-src separately (09-13 @ffa945f9, from 09-17 @6963ae9d)
  • aiter's CK/HIP JIT fails to compile at import on gfx1250, but the Triton ops are still usable
tune_attention.py --impl aiter; OE: output/0911__fa_gfx1250_phase1/E2.md §3
torch flex
  • Ships with torchtitan; no changes needed
  • Autotune is mandatory on B0 (num_warps=4). The fwd+bwd total of 37.122 ms (fwd 6.894 / bwd 30.228, B0 GPU0 2356 MHz) measured with the default heuristic (bwd num_warps=8) is retracted; after autotune the total is 15.26–15.31 ms
0914__repro__c07/flex_ledger.jsonl:1-3; 0914__repro__c07/RESULTS.md:89-98
aiter ASM fwd (fmha_bf16_pertokenBf16_hd128_128x256_mask.co)
  • Swapped in by the dispatch layer when aiter is importable; ASM fwd must be enabled together with the fused bwd (conjunctive gate)
  • Added a hard-off switch PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_DISABLE_ASM_FWD
  • Wrote a launcher that calls the .co directly
  • Installed psutil in the container; the first call JIT-compiles C++ and needs hipcc
0e5cb743, c93057b6, 20f2ccde, e4efebe2 (all on dev/lhz/attn, not upstreamed)
aiter ASM bwd (odo → bwd_hd128_bf16_causal_br_a32_pssk → dq_convert)
  • aiter's Python entry point does not select gfx1250; wrote our own ~674-line launcher, with the kernarg field table generated from the ELF and self-checked
  • dkdv_heads='q' plus a host reduction to avoid the GQA out-of-bounds write
  • Eligibility gate seqlen ≥ 2048; non-causal allowed
  • scratch reuse; HipModule made a process-wide singleton
  • Off by default (enabled only with PRIMUS_TURBO_ATTN_ENABLE_ASM_BWD=1)
  • op-evolve's beat arm loads _asm_bwd_kernargs.py by file path and does not import primus_turbo
  • c871cd47, adc367f0, c765edd4, 46b1774a, f8155ae9, b281b46b, 514e4a21, cb3ed519
  • tools/gfx1250/asm_bwd_{abi,launcher}.py
  • OE: …/job_context/op/beat/impl.py
aiter FlyDSL fwd m32x8
  • Requires flydsl 0.3.2: the ast_rewriter of 0.2.4 does not accept a list as a state variable
  • 0.3.2 is side-installed to ~/.local/flydsl032. 0.3.2 removed flydsl.expr.buffer_ops, so it cannot share a process with turbo's 0.2.4 tree
  • Switched to 0.3.4.1 from 09-25; performance unchanged
234ec33a (--impl flydsl), 963e7d7d; 0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md §4
Our FlyDSL fwd / bwd
  • op-evolve jobs: bwd pinned to 0.3.2, fwd uses 0.3.4.1
  • Product branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt (based on origin/main 8cda13c7) ported to the repo-pinned flydsl 0.2.4
  • The LLVM in 0.2.4 miscompiles the address of the async LDS→global O store; switched to buffer_store, at a cost of fwd −1.3%, bwd −1.8%
5658a443, e05b67fd, 5a8446b5, c36cc124; primus_turbo/flydsl/attention/gfx1250/
e2e integration (shared by all backends)
  • Accept enable_gqa. Previously the training config used converters: [] to dodge a TypeError, so turbo attention never entered e2e
  • pip uninstall primus_turbo inside the container: the editable 0.4.1.dev12 in the image shadowed the checkout
  • HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH points at the image library (the library/gfx1250 subdirectory)
  • nkfix works around bad hipBLASLt NN/wgrad tiles; rewritten on B0 as nkfix_b0.py + transpose_triton.py
  • e2e backend-switch shim (E2E_ATTN, no changes to Primus / Turbo source)
1babf4ce, 4fec7712, 23fdc0bc, f08ccffe; 0927__b0/gemm/, 0927__b0/e2e/, 1002__e2e/; WT: docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/

Overview: latest comparable readings per backend

For each backend only its most recent epoch with an ASM comparison is listed (epoch definitions in the E1–E5 table above). Absolute values in different rows come from different epochs and cannot be compared across rows; look only at each row's own "vs ASM". TF/s uses the op_flops nominal convention, and values marked * are converted from times; o1 / o2 are the two arm orders of the blocked ruler; e2e is 32 layers by default, and ms values marked † are converted from tps; ratios labeled "cross-tool", "cross-ruler" or "cross-process" indicate the order of magnitude only. Cells with no reading at all say "not measured". Main gaps:

  • E5 (after the reflash) measured only ASM, three FlyDSL variants (aiter m32x8, our champion, the product port) and main Triton; flex, aiter Triton and the vendored fused Triton have no E5 numbers.
  • After nkfix, flex and aiter Triton have no e2e numbers; turbo Triton has only an estimate on A0, and its only actual measurement is a single one on B0 in E4.

Overall speedup of the product branch over the main Triton dense backend (E5, A0, flydsl 0.2.4, bench_attention_turbo.py, 09-30): geometric mean over the 36 shapes admitted by the FlyDSL gate (causal 18 + non-causal 18) gives causal fwd 4.13× / bwd 3.37×, non-causal fwd 3.47× / bwd 3.58× (0930__port/PR_BODY.md:36-41; recomputing from the two CSVs gives the same result).

Caveat when citing these two bench CSVs: the bench's built-in reference is itself faulty on non-causal s8192; every backend reports dq ≈12 dB and is marked FAIL (TRITON 15 rows; FLYDSL 9 rows, and FLYDSL has another 54 rows that are ERROR because the gate does not admit them), whereas a CPU fp32 reference gives 52 dB. These FAILs are a problem with the reference, not kernel errors (0930__port/runs/bench_turbo_triton.log:48, bench_turbo_flydsl.log:72; session fdc2534d .txt:474).

Key points:

  • Triton family: the best Triton bwd (aiter tuned version, vendored fused version plus ITT) is still about 2–2.4 times ASM on throttled A0. Of these, ≈2.30–2.40× is a cross-ruler ratio: the E1 tune ruler (including ~1.4 ms of autograd) against the E3 benchmark.py bar of 7.68 ms; 2.01× is a same-process ratio, but its denominator is the bring-up ASM reading of 8.661 ms, which is on the slow side. The best fwd is about 1.5 times (E2 aiter Triton tuned upper bound ≈1.49×, same machine, cross-session). The gap is structural: a single ASM workgroup uses the full 320 KiB of LDS and 1024 VGPRs, and the bwd has 864 v_wmma instructions, while Triton allocates only 64 KB of LDS. Tuning Triton's parameters cannot close this gap (0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md).
  • fwd sensitivity to the clock: in the throttled-A0 era the fwd gap was amplified by the clock. The aiter FlyDSL fwd to ASM ratio was 1.51–1.53× on throttled A0 and dropped to 1.125× after the reflash. The reason is that ASM fwd barely changes with sclk, whereas FlyDSL scales with sclk.
  • An op-level lead does not necessarily carry over to training: at the training operating point (after a GEMM burst, ~1.28 GHz), s6 goes from 2.8% ahead to 3.8% behind, and fwd r16 goes from 8% behind to 35% behind.

b.3 e2e performance (Llama-3.1-8B)

Common configuration (unless otherwise noted): BF16, single card, MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192 (32,768 tokens per step), AC none, torch.compile off, 32 layers. "8L" is an 8-layer trimmed version, used only for the A0 09-15/16 GEMM ladder.

Conventions:

  • tokens/s is per-card throughput.
  • TFLOP/s is torchtitan's self-reported value; values marked † are converted from tps using the torchtitan convention (32L ~57.9 GFLOP/token, 8L ~16.84).
  • torchtitan's MFU uses the A100's 312 TF/s as the denominator, so it is entirely unusable on this card.
