e2e key points:

  • Before nkfix, e2e was choked by GEMM; attention was only a small slice of each step, and backend differences were essentially invisible:
    • A0 32L rocBLAS: under 1%. The 09-13 run (nominally turbo Triton, most likely actually flex), computed as 35.7 ms/layer × 32 = 1.14 s, is ~0.85% (0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md:266); the 09-15 ASM path, computed as 11.7 ms/layer × 32 ≈ 374 ms, is 0.28% (0915__opt/RESULTS.md:169), but the ASM bwd inside that 11.7 ms used the later-retracted shim reading of 10.160 ms, so the actual share is lower;
    • A0 8L hipBLASLt image library: 3.3% (measured with a profile, 0915__opt/BOTTLENECK-SHIFT.md:14);
    • B0 32L: 1.4–5.4% (0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md:37);
    • A0 09-13: turbo and flex differ by 0.4%; B0 09-28: FlyDSL vs ASM is 1.0045.
  • After nkfix, attention's share of each step:
    • turbo Triton 43.9% (B0);
    • ASM 17.0–17.4% (B0), 14.9% (A0);
    • FlyDSL 20.5–21.8% (B0), 15.3% (A0, s6).
  • Evolution of the FlyDSL-to-ASM per-step ratio: on B0, r6+r20 is 1.0475–1.0500, r13ns+r20 is 1.0403–1.0408, r16+r29 is 1.0323–1.0328; on A0 10-02, r16+r29 is 1.027 and r16+s6 is 0.999. Within the r16+s6 combination:
    • bwd is ~4 ms per step faster than ASM: the median of adjacent-pair differences is 4.50 / 4.56 ms (two processes), i.e. the 4.5 in the body text of RESULT-e2e.md; subtracting the two arms' own medians gives 3.8–4.4 ms (166.5 vs 162.2 / 162.6 in the RESULT-e2e.md table, i.e. 3.9–4.3).
    • fwd is 9.2 ms per step slower (adjacent pairs 9.18 / 9.24). Looking at attention alone, the remaining gap is in fwd.
    • What does not add up: summed over attention (fwd+bwd), fly takes ~4.3–5.2 ms more per step, yet the step is 0.7–2.0 ms faster. In other words, everything outside attention takes ~6 ms less per step on the fly arm (step − FA in e2e/runs/analysis.1002_095504.txt is −6.3 ms in both processes; flyr29 vs asm is −21 / −22 ms). Where outside attention this difference arises, and how much of it is noise, has not been broken down.
  • The product branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt still ships bwd r29 (1.235× ASM at op level under flydsl 0.2.4); s6 has not been ported into it yet.
  • The tutorial's e2e number cannot be compared directly with ASM: the tutorial's 1,361.9 ms/step is NKFIX_CHECK=0, while ASM's 1,350.6 ms is the 10-02 NKFIX_CHECK=1. The two differ by only 0.8%, but this does not mean the branch is only 0.8% slower than ASM. Adjusting for the ~33 ms/step that CHECK=1 adds (a value measured from a B0 trace and reused by the tutorial; 0927__b0/gemm/REPORT.md §5), ASM under CHECK=0 is ~1,318 ms and the branch is ~1.03× of it, i.e. ~3% slower (estimated, not measured). This is consistent with the 1.027 vs ASM of the 10-02 r16+r29 (flydsl 0.3.4.1), which is also CHECK=1.
  • The PR body cites an old number from another machine: 0930__port/PR_BODY.md:43 says "step time is 1.032x that of the ASM kernels" without naming the machine. This is the B0 09-28 r16+r29 reading (1.0323 / 1.0328, flydsl 0.3.4.1 tree); on A0 10-02 the same combination is 1.027, and r16+s6 is 0.999 (1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md). The PR body needs to be updated.
  • Not comparable across machines: the e2e absolute values of B0 (ASM 1,579 ms) and post-reflash A0 (ASM 1,351 ms) cannot be compared directly; the platform and the training clock both differ (the B0 final r16+r29 runs are at 1,342 / 1,371 MHz, the n5/n6 runs at 1,383 / 1,394 MHz; A0 10-02 median ~1.50 GHz).
  • NaN correction: on A0 09-16, 21% of the nkfix runs hit NaN, so several numbers were corrected or voided: 38,043 → 37,746, +14.40% → +12.72%, 49,878 and 11,608 void. The B0 rewrite ran 8 times over 486 steps with 0 NaN.

b.4 Problems with aiter ASM, the best-performing backend

Problem Symptom / impact Our handling Source
bwd has no callable gfx1250 Python entry point
  • can_impl_fmha_v3_bwd only recognizes gfx942/gfx950; although the C++ host has 5 gfx1250 special cases, a call through aiter's normal entry point silently gets no ASM bwd
  • kernarg: dqdkdv is 704 B, 44 fields, each field 16 B aligned; odo is tightly packed and uses kernarg preloading, which cannot be confirmed by disassembly; the dq_convert header says 192 B, the ELF has 208 B; all strides are in bytes
  • Read the calling contract out of the ELF offline and wrote our own launcher of about 674 lines
  • The ABI self-check caught that the hand-copied field table had missed the last 7 fields (including mask_x/mask_y). For these two fields aiter itself passes uninitialized stack values on the mt=2 path; we pass 0, which is safe
  • odo is verified after pre-filling it with NaN
0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md §1.1; 0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md; skill: references/baselines.md §3.2
GQA out-of-bounds write (the most substantive correctness bug)The pssk grid is (kv_tiles, nhead_q, batch), yet it indexes the kv-sized dk/dv by q head, so with G=4, 4 WGs contend for the same tile without synchronization:
  • s=1024: dk/dv silently corrupted, SQNR about −0.3 dB, dq still 52.24 dB
  • s=256: the out-of-bounds access crosses a page and the process faults (nothing in dmesg, the card is not harmed)
  • Localization experiment (ratio=4, dk/dv indexed by kv head): dk −0.94 / dv −0.65 dB; all correct at ratio=1
  • s=8192 with dkdv_heads=kv: triggers GCVM_L2_PROTECTION_FAULT, queues are reset per process, no AC-cycle spent
dkdv_heads='q' plus host reduction:
  • dk/dv device memory 0.125 → 0.500 GiB, 1.254 GiB more at peak
  • Time: fp32-sum version +0.482 ms (5.2% of bwd); bf16-reduce version 0.2225 ms (2.88% of the bar)
  • dk/dv precision drops by 1.7–1.9 dB (50.55 / 51.09)
Vendor report filed		0917__flydsl/VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md:15-38; 0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md:52-67; GQA-WORKAROUND-COST.md:50-65; skill: references/baselines.md §3.3
Not drop-in: 3 kernels, and the fp32 dq_acc must be zeroed on every call
  • odo → pssk (514 buffer_atomic_add_f32 writing the fp32 dq_acc) → dq_convert (ts_dq=64)
  • Without zeroing you silently get garbage dq
  • 6 dispatches per call
  • Per layer, dq_acc is 536 MB and dk/dv are 268 MB each
  • On B0 e2e the adaptation overhead is 9.2 ms/step (GQA summation 3.84, dq_convert 2.27, zeroing 1.62, odo 1.44), plus 1 GiB of resident scratch
asm_backward() allocates and zeroes internally; scratch is cached per shape (1.000 GiB process-wide). FlyDSL bwd directly outputs bf16 for the hkv heads, with none of this overheadT2-ASM-BACKWARD-SPEC.md; 0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md §0; e2e/BUILD.md §0
Precompiled .co only: thin assets, not tunable, not modifiable
  • gfx1250 has 52 .co files in total (gfx950 has 1466); only 6 for bwd (gfx950 has 124)
  • bwd has only a32 + pssk, bf16, hd128, batch mode. The a16_psskddv used on MI355 does not exist on gfx1250; no varlen bwd (dispatch has only mode=0, seqstart is passed as nullptr); no fp16 / swa / group
  • fwd has hd64, hd128 and varlen, but hd128 has no sink variant
  • VGPR 1024, LDS 327,680 B and wg 1024 are all fully used
Only call and wrap it, never modify the assembly. Suggested asking aiter for psskddv / a16 variants (no reply seen). Missing varlen and unmaintainability are the direct reasons the FlyDSL project was started0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md §4; VENDOR-REPORT-aiter-gfx1250.md §2; 0915__opt/BACKEND-STRATEGY.md:24-48
Measurement artifact: 10.160 ms (retracted)
  • On 09-15 the _AsmFwdAsmBwd autograd shim in tune_attention.py called asm_backward without passing hip= / scratch=, so every call did 3× hipModuleLoad and a fresh allocation of ~1 GiB; it also included ~1.4 ms of autograd
  • The value was mislabeled as the "product path" and stayed in use until 09-24
  • The 541 TF/s, 11.726 ms/layer, 4.757×, 1.74× and "FlyDSL already at 0.92× bar" derived from it are all too high
Use the same-session measurement of the op-evolve beat arm as the reference: 7.6134 ms on 09-17, 7.6766 ms (n=224) / ~716 TF/s in the 09-24 census. FlyDSL's real progress at the time was 0.70–0.72×skill: references/baselines.md §0, §5; 0924__flydsl/DAY-SUMMARY.md; 0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:284-302
Product integration: reloaded on every backward, scratch reallocated again and again, tight device memory
  • The product path asm_dense_backward did not pass a HipModule, so 32L did 96 hipModuleLoad calls per step and never unloaded them; in addition, every call allocated its own dq_acc (fp32) and per-q-head dk/dv. The op-level harness sees neither of these
  • A fresh allocation of about 1 GiB per layer makes 32L e2e 6.78% slower (n=1) Retracted: the scratch key does not include the layer index, so once cached it is 1.000 GiB process-wide and does not grow with the layer count; at 32L without caching, reserved memory is bitwise identical in both arms (380.06 GiB); the 6.78% (1,858 vs 1,984 tps) is n=1 and falls within the mode spacing of the multimodal e2e noise measured later (1.8% / 6.8% between clusters, cross-run sd ~3.9%)
  • After adding the scratch cache, 32L (88% device memory) wedged with SIGBUS at step 3 (+1.38 GiB, right up against the 88.30% ceiling), costing 1 AC-cycle
HipModule changed to a process-wide singleton (514e4a21); scratch reused (b281b46b). After both fixes, 8L, seed 1234, n=9 per arm: ON vs OFF is −0.28% (0.15× sem), no measurable e2e difference; the eligibility gate therefore stays off by default0915__opt/E2E-AB.md:94-133,180-282; ASM-ATTENTION.md §1.4
~0.85 ms fixed overhead, so short sequences are actually slowerASM bwd takes 0.88 / 0.91 / 1.00 / 1.34 ms over s1024→8192 (16× the work); s1024 loses at every degree of parallelism (0.537–0.660×)The eligibility gate is set to seqlen ≥ 2048 (f8155ae9). But part of this overhead is precisely the per-call hipModuleLoad; after the fix the 9 support points need to be re-measured, which was never done0915__opt/RESULTS.md:99-124; E2E-AB.md:296-302
dq uses fp32 atomic accumulation, non-deterministic
  • 514 buffer_atomic_add_f32 SCOPE_DEV; dq run-to-run SQNR is 113.0 dB on fast and 98.0 dB on prod, not bitwise identical
  • When is_deterministic is requested, aiter returns −1 and falls back to CK
  • This conflicts with our "bitwise identical over 200 runs" contract and forces FlyDSL onto 7-GEMM, a structural factor of 1.386–1.408×
On 09-25 the determinism gate was split (c741fa8d): dk/dv must still be bitwise identical, dq changed to run-to-run SQNR ≥ 70 dB. FlyDSL s6's dq is bitwise identical run-to-run0925__flydsl/AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md §2; 0923__flydsl/hint.md h18 / h41; 0930__bwd/REPORT.md
fwd's LSE layout is incompatible with the in-tree two-kernel bwdASM fwd outputs a natural-log LSE of shape [B,Hq,Sq] and writes it even when return_lse=False; the in-tree two-kernel bwd reads a packed [B,Hq,2*Sq]. Mixing them raises no error, and the gradients go "smoothly wrong"A conjunctive gate: ASM fwd is enabled only together with the fused bwd, and the decision is stored in ctx (0e5cb743)0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/T7-ASM-FORWARD.md (b); 0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:124-126
Fragile dependency chain at the fwd entry point
  • fmha_fwd_with_sink_asm JIT-compiles C++ on first call (needs hipcc); architecture detection relies on rocminfo
  • Whether it can be enabled depends on the unrelated psutil: when the e2e container lacks psutil it silently falls back to Triton fwd, an op-level loss of 9.8%, and 51/51 tests still pass
  • AITER_LOG_LEVEL=ERROR swallows the banner, so in the B0 09-14 training ASM fwd was in fact never called
  • On 09-13 it hit import jax on A0, which was absent on B0 on 09-14; cause not verified
Lazy one-time import, permanently disabled on failure; psutil added to the container; the gate itself writes a trace file to prove it was called; added a hard-off switch 20f2ccde for A/B0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:116-130,267-293; T7-ASM-FORWARD.md (a)
Version conflict, cannot serve as an upstream product backend
  • aiter pins flydsl 0.3.2 and Primus-Turbo pins 0.2.4; the two cannot live in the same process
  • aiter's gfx1250 dispatch never reaches ASM; it can only be launched by hand via ctypes
  • The test image has no aiter: on the product branch the 28 tests that fall back to aiter fail
Side-installed ~/.local/flydsl032 and flydsl0341; the beat arm loads by file path. The product branch dev/lhz/llama31_attn_opt now ships FlyDSL instead, keeping ASM only as a control arm0917__flydsl/STAGE1-FWD.md §4; 0930__port/PR_BODY.md Testing; session fdc2534d 2026-10-05
causal / layout / shape restrictions
  • bwd has only two kinds of .co: causal bottom-right (causal_br, CSV mask=2) and non-causal
  • The gate only admits self-attention: with Sq=Skv the two mask semantics are equivalent; cross-attention is rejected
  • Requires BSHD, a contiguous last dimension and hq%hkv==0; no support for sink, sliding window, bias, alibi, dropout or fp16 (the fwd gate has 12 rejection conditions in total)
  • aiter has only tested gqa=8 on hd128
Gate by capability. gqa=4 verified on B0: SQNR for all 32 q heads is within 53.54–53.72 dB. Non-causal admitted, a 1.71× speedup0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:118-128,273-275; 0915__opt/PROGRESS-REPORT.html Q3
Small dk/dv precision marginIn the host reduction each partial is first rounded to bf16, giving dk/dv 50.2–51.0 dB (prod 50.61 / 50.83) against a 50 dB threshold; the deterministic two-kernel version gets 52.29 / 52.70Accepted. FlyDSL reduces inside the kernel and reaches 52.4–52.6 dBAITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md §2; 0915__opt/TWOKERNEL-SWEEP.md:76-78
Clock behavior: the bar looks harder at low clocks, and ASM does not slow down at the training operating point
  • ASM fwd barely changes with sclk: 1.552 ms on A0 at 1100 MHz vs 1.396 ms on B0, only 1.11× apart (the same-code Triton bwd differs by 2.01×); on B0, 1350/2350 MHz gives 0.97×; after the reflash the clock is ×1.75 but ASM fwd is only 1.24× faster
  • PMC shows ASM fwd running at the power limit, with an effective in-kernel clock of only 1.36 GHz
  • Hence in the throttled era FlyDSL fwd looked 1.29–1.53× slower, but only 1.08× slower after the reflash
  • At the training operating point ASM stays essentially unchanged while FlyDSL slows down
Use same-process ratios only; added the gb ruler (measured after a GEMM burst); e2e is authoritative; reducing FlyDSL's clock sensitivity listed as a next step0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md; 0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md §2.1; 0930__roofline/REPORT.md; 1002__e2e/RESULT-realab.md
Readings swing sharply with the launch pattern, and a ruler can make the wrong pick
  • Same-process ASM fwd: 1.438 ms for 20 back-to-back runs, 1.266 with a sync after each call, 1.225 when fwd is immediately followed by bwd; under profile, iso / blk / eburst / layer give 1.22 / 1.45 / 1.13 / 1.00 ms respectively
  • Per-call interleaved timing reads FlyDSL vs ASM systematically low: fwd by ~25%, bwd by ~3.4%
  • The fast shape is launch-bound (bwd beat 0.089 ms) and pulls the geometric mean up, which caused B0 bwd r27 to be misjudged as target_met
Switched to blocked timing (h40 / h66); the criterion changed to proxy and prod each ≥ beat (h69)0927__b0/e2e/BUILD.md §3; ruler/REPORT.md; OP-EVOLVE-SUGGESTIONS.md #13
The toolchain cannot see everything
  • llvm-objdump cannot decode TDM instructions (opcode 0x31, shown as .long 0xd031…), so ASM fwd's prefetch depth can only be inferred
  • rocprofv3's VGPR_Count shows only half (512, actually 1024)
  • gfx1250 .co files at one point "could not be disassembled"
  • Decode descriptors by dword; read .vgpr_count from the ISA
  • On 09-25 got the bwd disassembly with --mcpu=gfx1250: 864 v_wmma, 514 atomics, 26 barriers
  • After the 09-29 reflash ATT works and shows per-instruction stalls
0925__flydsl/fwd-isa/REPORT.md; 0930__bwd/notes/report_asm.md; AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md §1
ASM itself is also far from the roofline (which means it can be beaten)
  • PMC: the bwd main kernel takes 7.71e6 cyc/SIMD, reaching only 34% of the 5-GEMM matrix lower bound
  • Only 1 wave per SIMD; per step, TDM waits cost 227 cyc, split barriers 208 cyc and atomic back-pressure 430 cyc
  • fwd reaches 72% of its lower bound, but the effective clock is only 1.36 GHz
s6 borrows the TDM 3-stage LDS ring and reads the next iteration's B operand into registers early; bwd reaches 103.8% of ASM0930__roofline/REPORT.md; 0930__bwd/notes/report_asm.md; 0930__bwd/REPORT.md §2
An undiagnosed card-state degradationDuring T7 on 09-14, ASM fwd at one point lost its edge entirely (turbo only 13% slower); it has not reproduced since and was never diagnosedStability under training load has not been confirmed separately0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:128-130
Wrong attributions circulating in the docs
  • "On B0, ASM bwd has no edge over fused (8.840 vs 8.848)" is wrong: both arms were fused Triton bwd; B0 never ran ASM bwd on 09-14
  • "A0/B0 clocks differ by 2.1× but attention only by 1.15×, so it is hardly clock-bound" holds only for ASM fwd: the bwd term compared A0's shim 10.160 with B0's fused 8.835
  • The 0914 report.html labeled the aiter Triton row as "hand-written ASM"
This section always relies on same-code, same-process ratios. Note that skill: references/baselines.md §8 has not been corrected yet: it still says "On B0 ASM bwd showed no edge over fused Triton (8.840 vs 8.848)" and treats B0's 8.835 as ASM bwd with shim overhead; it needs updatingskill: references/baselines.md:272-293 (the erroneous original text); 0914__campaign/HANDOFF.md:436-450 (§15: the --impl asm and --impl fused arms were in fact the same path); 0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:103 (--impl asm = ASM forward + fused backward); 0915__repro__c07/RESULTS.md

Summary

  1. Op-level ranking: multiples are comparable only within the same epoch, so they are given per epoch below; multiples from different epochs are not ranked on the same line.
    • bwd, E5 (A0 after the reflash, blocked, same-process): FlyDSL s6 0.963–0.972× (op-evolve job champion r24 ≈0.962× on the job ruler; 1.038× at the gb training operating point) ≈ aiter ASM > FlyDSL r29 1.20× (gb 1.342×). In the bench of the same period, factory-default main Triton dense is ~4.5× (cross-tool).
    • bwd, E1 (A0 throttled, tune_attention.py, all ≈ ratios against the E3 bar of 7.68 ms): in-tree vendored fused Triton plus ITT ≈2.30× (≈2.63× at 1cb2e183) ≈ aiter Triton tuned champion ≈2.40× > flex ≈3.1× > aiter Triton factory default ≈3.2–3.6× > turbo Triton tuned ≈4.2× > turbo Triton factory default ≈5.9–6.4× > SDPA (only a fwd+bwd total of 97.063 ms).
    • The two groups are bridged by FlyDSL readings on throttled A0: r20 in E3 ≈1.39× and r29 in E3′ 1.44×, both faster than the best Triton in E1 (≈2.3×). The clock state is the same and the rulers differ, but the two differ by about 6–7 ms, far more than the ~1.4 ms of autograd overhead in the E1 ruler. The B0 E4 factory-default turbo ≈3.4× comes from a cross-process opcheck and is not merged into the ranking above.
    • fwd: aiter ASM > FlyDSL r13ns (E5 1.08×, 1.35× at the gb training operating point; E4 1.08×; E3′ 1.29×) > aiter FlyDSL m32x8 (E5 1.13×; E1/E3/E3′ 1.50–1.53×) > aiter Triton tuned > turbo Triton tuned > flex > turbo Triton factory default. In E2 (B0 at full clock) / E1 (A0 throttled) the last four are 1.49× / 2.09×, 1.94× / 2.66×, 2.67× / 4.8× and 3.7× / 6.8× respectively, in the same order in both epochs. aiter Triton factory default has two mutually contradictory readings in E1 (≈4.3× / ≈2.4×) and is left out of the ranking.
  2. e2e: on A0 on 10-02, FlyDSL r16+s6 is on par with ASM (per step 0.999), and r16+r29 is 1.027 (same-process); on B0, the per-step time of factory-default turbo Triton is ~1.42× that of ASM (2,250 vs 1,582 ms, 14,563 vs 20,718 tps), but this is a cross-process ratio (turbo in a separate process, n7; ASM n5), not a same-process pairing like fly/asm. Before nkfix, differences between any attention backends were masked by GEMM.
  3. Why an in-house FlyDSL: ASM is the fastest, but it is a black box — no Python entry point, a GQA out-of-bounds write, only 6 .co files for bwd, no varlen, non-deterministic dq, a conflict with the flydsl version, and it can be neither modified nor tuned. Triton is limited by its LDS and register structure and at best reaches about 2× ASM's time. aiter's FlyDSL has only fwd and is 1.5× slower (a same-process reading on throttled A0 when the project started on 09-17; 1.13× after the reflash). So on 09-17 the project was started on the grounds of "maintainable, adds varlen, deterministic, upstreamable".
  4. Result: bwd has overtaken ASM (s6); fwd is still ~8% behind at op level and ~9 ms behind per step in e2e.

(c) Progress of each optimization round

This section first gives the main line in a milestones-at-a-glance table (c.2a), then arranges every champion change point from 09-10 to 10-05 chronologically in a master table (c.2), and finally gives the current best versions (c.3) and the key points of the progress (c.4). Per-round details, one row per round, are in rounds.xlsx (198 rows, not merged; sheet 2 lists the rounds with an improvement separately). Paths without a prefix are relative to Primus-Turbo/output/; OE: stands for /home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve/artifacts/.

Software stack: container fa-repro (image fa-tune:deps, i.e. amdprimus/amdprimus:gfx1250-20260910 plus the torchtitan dependencies), torch 2.11.0+rocm7.14.0a20260625, triton 3.6.0, aiter-src @6963ae9, torchtitan v0.2.2 (73a0e6979). Three flydsl versions coexist:

  • 0.2.4: ships with the image; the product branch is pinned to it;
  • 0.3.2: used by the A0 bwd job champion and the hand-campaign s1–s6;
  • 0.3.4.1: used by the fwd job, B0 and e2e since 09-25.

Rulers

  1. tune_attention.py (09-13..09-16): one layer of fwd+bwd, median of CUDA events, iters 20, a 256 MiB L2 flush every time; four-tensor SQNR gate (out/dq/dk/dv ≥50 dB). Reports fwd / bwd / total ms; TF/s is computed on the fwd+bwd total.
  2. op-evolve benchmark.py, per-call interleaving (the two A0 jobs 09-17..09-27; B0 fwd r5–r9; B0 bwd r24–r26):
    • Each arm takes the median of 51 calls, with palindromic interleaving; the ASM beat and the candidate run in the same process.
    • Three shapes: fast (1,1024,1024,8,2,128), proxy (1,4096,4096,32,8,128), prod (4,8192,8192,32,8,128); ranking is by prod only.
    • gain = the ratio of this round's code to the best-ever code, both re-measured in the same session. min_gain: for bwd it was 0 at first (changed to 0.007 on B0 on 09-28 and on A0 on 09-30); for fwd it is 0.007.
    • From r9 on, the fwd job no longer runs in the same process as the beat (h28). After r10 was rejected on a three-shape arithmetic mean of 1.0022, the operator added gain_weights (prod 1 / proxy 0.25 / fast 0), effective from r11 [a8fa47bf]. The A0 bwd job also added gain_weights from r25 on, and fast is now scored by min.
  3. Blocked timing (blocked, h40/h41/h66): B0 fwd from the act of r10 on (the act was already timed with blocked 9 + lead 4 at 09-28 03:16 UTC [0927__b0/fwd/rounds/010/act.md], whereas ruler/REPORT.md records the harness swap at ~03:40 UTC); B0 bwd from r27 on (r27 in progress.md already reports a blocked beat of 817.8, whereas ruler/bwd/REPORT.md records the install time as ~05:00 UTC). In both places the recorded install time is later than the actual use; the round is authoritative. A0 has used it ever since. Each arm first runs 4 untimed calls, then 9 consecutive timed calls, with palindromic order between passes. An audit found that per-call interleaving pushes the FlyDSL/ASM ratio down: by ~25% for fwd and ~3.4% for bwd. After switching to blocked timing, the A/A error is ±0.19% for fwd and ±0.05% for bwd.
  4. Real data and the training operating point (h47): the inputs are 6 layers of real q/k/v dumped at B0 step 43. "gb" means running 10 bf16 32768×4096×14336 GEMMs before each timing to pull the clock down to the training operating point (~1.28 GHz on A0). FlyDSL is clock-sensitive and ASM almost insensitive, so the FlyDSL/ASM ratio can differ by more than 25% between the randn/blk and real/gb conditions.
  5. e2e: Llama-3.1-8B BF16, 32 layers (some 09-15/16 experiments used 8 layers), MBS=GBS=4, seq 8192, 32,768 tokens per step, single card, no activation checkpointing, no compile; tps = tokens/s.
    • From 09-16 on, attention comparisons presuppose nkfix (rewriting the GEMM layout of aten::mm at runtime).
    • NKFIX_CHECK=1 costs ~33 ms extra per step (it was on in the 10-02 e2e and turned off in the 10-05 tutorial).
    • torchtitan's mfu column is computed against the A100 peak and is unusable.

FLOP convention

  • Current convention (op-evolve tools/op_flops.py): fwd 2.199292e12, bwd 5.498229e12 (5-GEMM nominal count, causal taken as (s+1)/(2s)), fwd+bwd 7.697e12; ms = FLOP / TF/s. Values marked derived in the tables are all converted this way.
  • TF/s in the 09-13..09-16 documents is the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 / total ms). The 0913/0914 documents also reported bwd on a 7-GEMM issued basis (e.g. B0 vendored fused bwd 748.4 vs 534.6 TF/s); this section does not use that convention. The 09-14 B0 Triton op-evolve job counts bwd only.
  • The 09-23 STAGE2-FWD-SWEEP uses fwd ≈2.233e12 (old harness; TF/s ~1.5% too high). The 5.8 TF/s of 09-17 DKDV-FIRST-TIMING is on a custom "useful" convention (single head) and is not comparable.
  • Our 1-wave bwd (r0 through s6) actually issues 7 GEMMs, 1.4076× the algorithmic amount; aiter ASM issues only 1.0155×. This section always counts the algorithmic amount. So s6's "103.8% of ASM" is achieved while doing ~39% more matrix math.

"% of ASM": ASM time ÷ this row's time (equal to this row's TF/s ÷ ASM TF/s). Same-process or same-session ASM measurements are preferred; above 100% means faster than ASM. Before 09-14 there was no ASM yet; recorded as "—". op-evolve rounds use that round's same-session beat.

Why numbers are not comparable across epochs (measured examples)

  • Same code, same ruler, only the platform changed (A0-T 09-28 → A0-R 09-30):
    • FlyDSL fwd r13ns 2.023→1.353 ms (order o1; reversed order o2: 1.992→1.356), a 1.47–1.50× speedup (both orders, mean ~1.48×); ASM fwd 1.564→1.257 (1.24×; 1.23× for o2);
    • FlyDSL bwd r29 11.010→6.583 (1.67×), ASM bwd 7.654→5.503 (1.39×).
    • Even the ratio changed: bwd r29 went from 69.5% of ASM to 83.6%.
    • At the e2e level there is no clean like-for-like factor. The 1,947→1,351 ms (1.44×) for ASM attention + nkfix in tutorial §7 is not purely a platform difference: 1,947 comes from the void 09-28 a0_p3a process, in which the same-process FlyDSL arm was loaded from step 2 on and the fwd tree's _env.py then repointed HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH to the host library (whether hipBLASLt re-reads this variable mid-run is unproven); 1,351 is the 10-02 reading (NKFIX_CHECK=1 on both sides), and between the two runs the driver also changed from 7.1.1-2397345 to 7.1.0-2412954 (via -2410994, -2411826, -2411946) [0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §2,§5.3,§6.3; 1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md §1].
  • Same machine and code, only the ruler changed: after B0 fwd switched from interleaved to blocked, % of ASM jumped from ~85% to ~96%; the kernel did not change. From r16 on, the champion r13ns reads ~4.8% low on randn by design.
  • Same code, only the inputs and operating point changed: A0 10-02, bwd s6/ASM time ratio blk 0.972, gb 1.038; fwd r16/ASM blk 1.081, gb 1.349.
  • Values drift across an AC cycle: A0 09-22, the same r7 code, 404.06 vs 382.73 TF/s (+5.6%). Same-session noise floor 0.24–0.66%, cross-session drift ~1.5%.

Round numbering

  • There are two sets of bwd rounds 24–27. One is r24–r32, run on B0 on 09-27/28, written "B0 r24" etc.; its ledgers are only in 0927__b0/bwd/. The other is r24–r27, resumed on A0 in the live directory on 09-30 and 10-02, written "A0 r24" etc.
  • The ledgers of A0 bwd job r0–r23 are identical entry by entry in OE:gfx1250-flydsl-attn-bwd-20260917-115934.a0-stale-0930/ and in the live directory; the 09-30 close-out of r23 is recorded only in the live directory.
  • fwd has two jobs: A0's gfx1250-flydsl-attn-fwd-20260925-114644 (r0–r13), and B0's gfx1250-flydsl-attn-fwd-b0-20260927 (cloned from A0's r4; ran r5–r20 on B0).
  • The per-shape champions pointers in state.yaml are not necessarily the same as best_round. A0 bwd job: best_round=24, champions={prod: 25, proxy: 25, fast: 24}; r25, although rejected, holds the best-ever record for prod/proxy. A0 fwd job: best_round=11, champions={prod: 12, proxy: 11, fast: 11}. On resume, the readings of these rejected rounds are used as the best-ever threshold; this is how B0 fwd's r9 and r19 were blocked.

c.2 Master table

Where a single row's "op time ms" is written as a / b / c, the values are fwd / bwd / total, in that order. Every number in the TF/s column is followed by its convention: "(total)" = 7.697e12 ÷ total fwd+bwd ms, which is how tune_attention and the Primus bench report it, and it is used throughout Stages 1–2 except #13; "(bwd)" and "(fwd)" are per direction, computed with 5.498229e12 and 2.199292e12 respectively, and the B0 Triton job in #13 and everything from Stage 3 on are per direction. Numbers on the total convention cannot be compared directly with per-direction numbers. Consecutive rejected rounds are merged into one row; per-round details are in rounds.xlsx.

6509-28fwd+bwdB0 in-training attention profile (e0e42ad7)Per layer in training (after nkfix): fly fwd 1.57–1.84 / ASM fwd 1.21–1.23; k_dkdv 5.95–5.99 + k_dq 3.30–3.35 / ASM bwd main kernel 7.00–7.09——fly (fwd r6 + bwd r20) is 87 ms per step slower than ASM: fwd +19.9, bwd +71.0, ASM-only GQA reduction −3.9 msB0-28 GPU0 (in training, attention runs at 1250–1690 MHz, whole board ~2.13 kW)Explains why the op-level conclusion flips in training: two factors multiply. ① Clock: on randn, FlyDSL fwd is 0.84× of ASM at 2350 MHz but 1.20× right after a GEMM (~1300 MHz); ASM fwd barely changes with clock. ② Data: on the real q/k/v dumped at step 43, score standard deviation 21–53, r6's speculative softmax falls back and recomputes in 13–25% of tile steps (0% on randn). With real inputs + post-GEMM clocks, op-level fly/asm is 1.45–1.67. The bwd gap is mainly in k_dq (3.33 ms/layer, ~107 ms/step; the fused ASM kernel has no such work)This led to the fwd nospec lab → h44 (r16 = r13ns); the subsequent bwd lab-kdq targeted k_dq → u2n (r29). The profile's characterization of k_dq (latency/memory bound, clock-insensitive) was later corrected by lab-kdq: the first round judged it issue bound, the second round measured k_dq as ~1.36× clock-sensitive at 1272–1352 MHz; this still does not reconcile with the profile's reading that k_dq is only 2% slower in training [0927__b0/profile/REPORT.md §0,§3; 0927__b0/lab-kdq/REPORT.md]

c.4 Key points of the progress curve

  • The top e2e lever is GEMM, not attention.
    • The hipBLASLt path fix took A0 32L from 244 to 2,027 tps (8.3×). nkfix then brought another 6.16× on A0 8L; on B0 32L the ASM arm reached 20,718 tps, 10.3× the pre-fix high tier of 2,016–2,022 tps (10.8× vs the low-tier median 1,922), with GEMM per step going 15.6–16.5 s→0.83–0.86 s, ~18–20×.
    • Before the fix, attention was only <1% to 3.3% of a step: on A0 09-13 32L via rocBLAS, the whole attention path was 35.7 ms × 32 = 1.14 s, ~0.85% (phase1/RESULTS.md:266); A0 8L via hipBLASLt without nkfix, 3.3% (BOTTLENECK-SHIFT); B0 09-28 before nkfix, 1.4–5.4% (0927__b0/e2e/RESULT.md:37). The e2e A/B of ASM bwd was therefore drowned in e2e noise (8L n=9, −0.28%, not significant). After the fix attention takes 15–22% (A0 8L 20.8%, B0 17–22%, A0-D 10-02 ~15%), and only then do op-level improvements become visible in e2e.
  • Triton stage: 2.44× in one day on A0, 2.75× in one day on B0.
    • On A0-T 59.642→24.435 ms, from two config knobs plus the vendored aiter fused bwd.
    • On B0-14, first reproduced the Day-1 ladder (28.121→12.994 ms), then got down to 10.235 ms with waves_per_eu, ASM fwd (fwd 1.82×) and in-thread transpose: 2.75× vs factory default.
    • waves_per_eu shows no measurable gain on the throttled machine but is worth 4.2% at full clock: the machine state itself decides which levers work.
  • ASM fwd is the largest single jump in the fwd direction (A0 4.253→1.549 ms, 2.74×) and has served as the bar ever since. The ASM bwd bar was at first overstated in time by 32% by a bogus 10.160 ms shim number (equivalent to understating ASM's speed); the 09-24 census set it at 7.6766 ms, and FlyDSL's "0.92×" was corrected to "0.70–0.72×" accordingly.
  • FlyDSL bwd from scratch to ~0.71× (A0-T, 20 rounds, 57.24→511.42 TF/s, 8.9×; 0.714 vs the 09-24 bar of 716 TF/s, 0.72 using r22's same-session re-measurement of 516.87).
    • Comparing prod-champion TF/s round over round (each round's reading in its own session), the four biggest jumps are r1 1.62× (57.24→92.62, causal tile skipping), r4 1.60× (207.47→330.98, LDS padding removing 64-way bank conflicts), r2 1.55× (address clamping + 32-deep contraction + BLOCK_KV 32) and r3 1.45× (k_dq BLOCK_Q 64).
    • The framework's three-shape gain ranks them differently: r2 1.921, r4 1.512, r13 1.370, r1 1.342, r3 1.297. r13's gain came entirely from fast/proxy; prod did not move.
    • After r12 came the efficiency phase, with the ceiling capped by the 7 vs 5 GEMM structural factor of 1.386×; r13–r23 added only ~2–3% on prod.
  • fwd gains come mainly from scheduling and softmax instructions.
    • On A0-T 935→1111 TF/s (+18.8%), made up of three items: longest-first scheduling, packed exp/row-sum, and nodelay.
    • On B0, speculative softmax gave +4.6% on randn but was actually slower on real data and at the training clock. After r16 turned speculation off (r13ns), real data got 8–15% faster, and real data plus the training clock 12–23% faster. The same change goes in opposite directions on the two rulers.
  • Corrections to rulers and criteria themselves "rewrote" part of the progress.
    • After B0 switched from interleaved to blocked, fwd % of ASM went from ~85% to ~96%. At the same time two false wins were retracted (lab2-L12 +6.4%→−0.4%, lab3-L21 +6.3%→~+0.3%), and fwd r6's +5.7% was revised down to +4.6% (still a real gain).
    • r15's arithmetic mean, B0 r27's geomean falsely flagging target_met, and A0 r24's fast-median false positive: all three framework criterion errors were corrected by hand.
  • The A0 firmware reflash made the same code 1.24–1.67× faster (o1 order; in the reverse order o2, ASM fwd 1.23× and FlyDSL fwd 1.47×), with FlyDSL benefiting more: bwd r29 rose from 69.5% to 83.6% of ASM, and fwd r13ns/ASM fell from 1.29 to 1.08. All A0 absolute numbers from before 09-29 are void. e2e has no clean same-convention ratio (the 1.44× is not pure, see c.1).
  • The bwd hand campaign went from 83.6% to 103.8% in one day (09-30, r29 6.58→s6 5.295 ms, −19.5%).
    • The core lever is the 3-stage TDM LDS ring, plus reading the next iteration's B operands back into registers early. Both steps are whole-op changes: swapping in dkdv_tdm3 for k_dkdv alone made the whole op −11.17% vs r29 (5.856 vs 6.592 ms); after k_dqg also moved to TDM (s5), the whole op went a further −2.1% vs s4. Ablation A2 first gave the ceiling, then ATT (available only after the reflash) pinned it down to specific instructions.
    • s6 still has the 7-GEMM structure, doing ~39% more matrix math, and is still faster than ASM.
    • The 5-GEMM fused version w4f needs only 4.241 ms without dQ atomic accumulation (relax_noatom) (129.6% of ASM; output wrong, ceiling only), but dQ atomic writes eat ~5 ms, so it is paused.
  • e2e went from 4.8–5.0% behind to parity.
    • On B0 fly/asm 1.0475 (r6+r20)→1.0454 (swap in fwd r13, still speculative)→1.0403 (swap in fwd r13ns)→1.0323 (swap in bwd r29). On A0-D, switching further to s6 gives 0.999, saving 53 ms of bwd per step. Absolute ms on B0 and A0-D are not comparable; only same-process ratios are compared here.
    • The remaining gap is in fwd: r16/ASM 1.349 at the training operating point, 9.2 ms slower per step.
    • The product branch is still on r29; s6 has not been ported into it yet. The same combination (fwd r16 + bwd r29) was 1.027× of ASM in #81, but that was a flydsl 0.3.4.1 build; the branch itself (0.2.4) has only been measured in the tutorial e2e at 1,361.9 ms/step, which is NKFIX_CHECK=0; converted to the same convention with +33 ms/step it is ~3% slower than ASM (estimated, not measured). Next steps are fwd, and switching the scoring ruler to the training-operating-point gb ruler (designed 10-02, not installed).

(d) Problems encountered along the way and their solutions

Reading conventions

  • Machines and clock states (absolute numbers from different states cannot be compared directly; wherever they appear side by side, this is stated separately):
    • A0-throttled: heliosr-1b114-c07-1 (single card), before the 2026-09-29 firmware reflash; VR throttle, DPM has only two levels, 500/1100 MHz; 1100 MHz idle, ~0.95–1.07 GHz under load.
    • A0-new-firmware: the same card, after the 09-29 reflash of VBIOS 630A→700E and SMU 125.7.1→125.12.0. 1.74–2.03 GHz at op level, ~1.5 GHz during training (2,500 W power limit). From 10-01 the driver is amdgpu-dkms 7.1.0-2412954.
    • B0: ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1, 4×gfx1250, 2,133–2,244 MHz under load. ~1.25–1.69 GHz during training after the 09-28 nkfix.
  • How sources are written:
    • Paths omit the prefix output/.
    • s:<first 8 chars of session id> <time Z> refers to a session transcript.
    • OE: refers to the op-evolve repository /home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve.
    • skill: refers to .claude/skills/gfx1250-attn-campaign/references/*.md or ~/.claude/skills/*.
    • An 8-digit hex string is a Primus-Turbo commit.
    • hNN is a hint number: A0 bwd in 0923__flydsl/hint.md, A0 fwd in 0925__flydsl/fwd-job/hint.md, B0 in 0927__b0/{fwd,bwd}-hint.md, and A0 after 09-30 (h83/h85) in the bwd job's job_context/hint.md.
  • All times are UTC. There are two sets of bwd round numbers r24–r27: one on B0 09-27/28 and one on A0 09-30/10-02. This section always states the machine.

d.1 GPU wedges and AC power cycles

On both machines, once a card is wedged for good (a wedge), no software means can bring it back: the driver's own reset cannot complete, the container holds unkillable D-state processes, and the reference count of /dev/kfd never drops to zero. The only way to recover is for the user to go to the machine in person and cut power to the whole box (an AC power cycle). Wedges are therefore first a scheduling constraint, and only second a technical problem.

d.1.1 Incident table (deduplicated across sources)

Class legend:

  • A: out-of-bounds address write by a reference GEMM (fp32 Tensile), i.e. the "memory out-of-bounds" class.
  • B: TLB/queue class. The first line is an INVALIDATE_TLBS timeout, with no memory fault of any kind before it.
  • C: an out-of-bounds read by a candidate kernel sets off a fault storm, accompanied by IH ring buffer overflow.
  • S: startup-time MES fault family, unattributed.
  • K: known operational triggers, including online tuning, inductor, bad candidates, the profiler, and new kernels sent straight to prod.
  • R: resource or process problems, such as SIGBUS, leftover KFD state, or something holding the card.
  • F: process-level fault, recoverable, not counted as a wedge.
  • D: degraded, not wedged for good.
  • X: non-kernel causes, such as the driver, firmware, or coordination with others.

d.1.2 Wedge classes and how our understanding evolved

  1. 09-04..09-13 (A0-throttled): first treated as a platform problem.
    • Four unrelated workloads all triggered the same kind of wedge: MES unresponsive, reset unable to complete. The four workloads were HipKittens GEMM, grouped-GEMM, the E3 mask outer product, and E4 PC sampling.
    • The conclusions at the time: the card itself is fragile; PC sampling always wedges; reloading the driver is not feasible, because D-state processes do not release /dev/kfd and the amdgpu refcount never drops to zero.
    • It was also found that during a wedge, rocm-smi, ps -eo …wchan, pgrep and docker exec all hang themselves.
    • Action: filed HARDWARE-ISSUE / PLATFORM-ESCALATION and moved to B0 on 09-14.
  2. 09-14..09-17: learned to tell three states apart.
    • "Degraded" is not "wedged for good". B0 GPU0 had 120 MES failed to respond lines but could still run kernels to completion. Only wait for reset ack, ring gfx timeout and GPU reset begin mean unrecoverable.
    • A "process-level fault" is not a "wedge". The Queues reset on process on 09-17 did not need an AC.
    • There were many startup wedges during e2e work: on 09-16, 3 of the day's 14 launches wedged (21%), from which it followed that risk is priced by the number of launches, not by run duration.
    • Confirmed triggers identified: autotune inside the training step, converters: []→inductor, Triton num_warps=16, bad candidate #58, starting a new run while KFD leftovers remain, modprobe -r, repeated SIGKILL of running kernels, and a "CPU-only" agent going onto the card on its own.
    • Another conclusion: SIGBUS is a symptom, not the cause.
  3. 09-21..09-24 (op-evolve bwd job): split into three classes A/B/C by the first dmesg line; the discriminants were falsified one by one.
    • Four "wedge precursor" discriminants were proposed in turn, and all were overturned by measurement:
      • h15: truncated sub-4GB address + PF 0x5/RW 0x1 + copy_context_work_handler. Class B wedges never trigger it at all.
      • h22: IH ring buffer overflow. During the 09-24 boot there were 8 overflows and the card stayed alive, while the 08:58 wedge had no overflow at all.
      • MORE_FAULTS / multiple XCDs. 8/8 of the survivable batches had MORE_FAULTS.
      • A fault arrival rate of "6000×". That number is actually the driver's ~97 ms throttling (7436cd29).
    • Conclusion (3fd48059): there is no usable wedge predictor; alert only on the wedge itself.
    • Two triggers were actually pinned down in this period: first, the fp32 Tensile reference that the gate ran on the card, solved with refcache; second, running multiple shapes in a single process, which faulted in ~44% of processes; after switching to one shape per process, 6 consecutive runs had 0 faults.
    • In the same period it was also found that dmesg_restrict=1 had invalidated all earlier "zero faults" checks.
  4. 09-28..10-06: the host library and new kernels became the main triggers; the firmware reflash did not make the card any sturdier.
    • The host hipBLASLt library ~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250 is associated with several incidents: on A0 on 09-28, one NaN and one wedge; B0 stuck at step 1; the 10-02 wedge.
    • Running the fp32 reference GEMM on the card still causes faults (B0 GPU2).
    • After the 09-29 reflash the clock recovered and ATT became usable, but on 10-02 a new kernel sent straight to prod still wedged the card. On 10-06, hipblaslt-bench wedged it too when its enumeration hit a bad Tensile solution.
    • The rules converged into h85: scale new kernels up step by step from toy→prod; probes that only read ISA/VGPR are always compile-only; use only the hipBLASLt library shipped in the image.
    • Another data point: B0 had ≥12 training launches on 09-27/28 with 0 wedges. Pooling all periods, the startup wedge rate is about ≤5/31≈16% (1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md:377).

d.1.3 Countermeasures that worked

Measure Failure addressed Introduced Evidence / effect Source
No driver-level recovery: modprobe -r amdgpu removed from the recovery ladder. Recovery steps after an AC (gfx1250-card-safety §5, 1002__oe/incident/WEDGE-1002.md):

# 0. The user does the AC power cycle (only this step recovers a wedge; never modprobe -r)
sudo modprobe amdgpu                    # kernel cmdline has modprobe.blacklist=amdgpu, so it does not load automatically
sleep 6
sudo sysctl -w kernel.dmesg_restrict=0  # reverts to 1 on every reboot; confirm dmesg | wc -l shows thousands of lines
cat /sys/class/drm/card*/device/pp_dpm_sclk   # check whether someone changed the DPM table/VBIOS/dkms again
ls /sys/class/kfd/kfd/proc/             # must be empty
docker start fa-repro                   # reuse, do not rebuild; confirm the container has no image-shipped primus_turbo
# one 4096^3 bf16 matmul with a timeout (A0-throttled ~5.07 ms) -- this is what counts as "the card works", rocm-smi does not
git fsck --no-dangling; find .git/objects -size 0   # a power cut may truncate git objects
# add the hint first (e.g. h85), then op-evolve resume (without --config)

d.2.2 What nkfix is and how to install it

  • Problem: the hipBLASLt in the image ships pure-bf16 tuned libraries only for the TN layout (Alik_Bljk) used by the forward Linear. The backward dgrad (Ailk_Bljk) and wgrad (Ailk_Bjlk) can only look up the sparse GridBased table, whose nearest neighbor lands at N=1, so they end up with the GEMV tile MT32x16x32 at only 50–80 TF/s. The forward reaches 1.5–1.9 PF/s, an 11–32× gap per call.
  • Approach: intercept aten::mm with TorchDispatchMode and change the physical layout of the backward GEMM operands to the forward one, so that they hit MT256x256x128:
    • dgrad, when B is contiguous, becomes mm(A, B.t().contiguous().t()).
    • wgrad becomes mm(A.contiguous(), B.t().contiguous().t()). The wgrad criterion must be checked before the dgrad one.
  • Approaches that did not work:
    • The zero-copy form (BᵀAᵀ)ᵀ still lands on the bad tile, only 0.83–0.95×, because this library has good tiles for the TN layout only.
    • Overriding aten::mm with torch.library recurses infinitely.
    • Putting it in sitecustomize fails because torch is not loaded yet at that point.
  • Numerics: op-level SQNR is identical to the original call, 55.59–55.63 dB in both cases; over 99.996% of elements differ by at most 1 ulp; bitwise deterministic across 3 repeats.
  • Versions:
    • A0 v1 (09-15) changes dgrad only.
    • A0 v3 (09-16) adds wgrad; rule 3 is the FlyDSL wgrad, driven by an offline table and off by default.
    • B0 nkfix_b0.py (09-28) is a rewrite on top of 1255557f:
      • Persistent scratch, keyed by (device, stream).
      • Chunked along the output dimension, chunk size NKFIX_CHUNK_BYTES defaulting to 256 MiB; the K reduction is not split.
      • Transposes use the blocked Triton kernel transpose_triton.py, bitwise identical to the torch copy and 5–7× faster.
      • NKFIX_CHECK=0/1/2 checks for non-finite values; NKFIX_SHADOW runs a shadow comparison during real training.
    • The tutorial uses exactly this B0 version.
  • Installation:
    • Tutorial route: primus-nkfix-hook.patch adds 14 lines to _run_trainer_lifecycle in Primus's primus/core/runtime/train_runtime.py, which import nkfix and call install() when NKFIX_ENABLE=1.
    • B0 e2e kit route: the shim's primus_turbo/__init__.py, together with E2E_NKFIX=1.
    • The Primus-side hook from A0 09-15/16 is backed up only in 0915__opt/primus-side/ and was never pushed.
    • transpose_triton.py must be placed in the same directory as nkfix.py.
  • Overhead: ~70 ms per step; NKFIX_CHECK=1 adds another ~33 ms/step.

d.2.4 NaN, HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH and host-library risks

  1. nkfix and NaN (A0-throttled, 09-16)
    • Runs with nkfix hit NaN in 8/39 cases, runs without in 0/35 (p=0.004). A separate count: with nkfix, 7 of 40 runs showed only grad_norm=inf; without, 3 of 36.
    • All NaNs occurred at step ≤10 and were entangled with that day's MES TLB faults. The root cause was never found.
    • The B0 rewrite: 8 nkfix runs in total, 486 steps (150 unstable steps), 0 NaN. The upper bounds are computed only over the 7 runs at 32 layers among them, 466 steps (138 unstable steps): the 95% upper bound per step is 2.2%/step; per run it is 35%/run, not yet enough to rule out A0's 21%/run; pushing it below 21% would take ~13 clean runs (0927__b0/gemm/REPORT.md §0 §4).
    • Hence nkfix stays opt-in; turn on NKFIX_CHECK=1 for long runs or debugging; any NaN or inf voids that run's speed data in its entirety.
  2. Library path
    • The default search path is one directory level too high: library/ contains only a single gfx1250/ subdirectory, which holds all 402 files, so the result is INVALID_VALUE. The 09-23 S0-b directory table did not list this subdirectory, which is why the host library was adopted instead (see d.2.1).
    • _rocm_sdk_devel/.../library/gfx1250 is an incomplete copy (326 files, no Kernels.so-000, no extop/transform) and gives SIGSEGV, exit 139.
    • /opt/rocm/lib/hipblaslt is an empty directory; the container's ROCm is not under /opt/rocm at all.
    • Falling back to rocBLAS makes e2e 8× slower, and its fp32 Tensile kernel has also caused page faults on the card.
  3. TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT is silently rewritten, in at least three places
    • sitecustomize.py uses setdefault("0"); we added this ourselves on 09-17.
    • /etc/profile.d/zz-gfx1250.sh exports it as 0; this only takes effect in a login shell, so bash -lc gets caught.
    • tune_attention.py sets it to 0 itself before importing torch.
    • Rules: assign only, never setdefault; use bash -c; confirm by printing torch.backends.cuda.preferred_blas_library() inside the process.
    • Also, runtime.env in the op-evolve spec does not actually take effect; the setting must go into the op's own code or the exec command line.
  4. Host library ~/.local/hipblaslt-gfx1250 (a copy from host ROCm 10.1.0, 328 files)
    • Validated only on square matrices.
    • Associated with these incidents: the B0 hang at step 1 (p1a/p1b single-variable control, n=1 per side); GEMM bursts ~20× slower; the A0 09-28 NaN and wedge; the 10-02 wedge. Apart from p1a/p1b, all of this is correlational evidence only.
    • Banned by h85 since 10-02.
    • skill:env-and-pitfalls §1b still recommends the host library to this day; it is outdated and needs updating.
  5. fp32 GEMM reference on the card: causes class-A wedges (refcache) and the B0 GPU2 fault (h50). References for tests and gates are always computed on the CPU or served from refcache; a refcache sha mismatch is an immediate FAIL (see d.5).
  6. hipblaslt-bench --algo_method all: hits bad precompiled solutions and wedges the card; do not use it.

d.2.5 Currently recommended configuration (10-05 tutorial, measured on A0-new-firmware)

# Patch the nkfix hook into Primus: git apply docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/primus-nkfix-hook.patch
# Inside the container: pip uninstall -y primus_turbo (the editable install in the image shadows the checkout); do not use REBUILD_PRIMUS_TURBO=1
docker exec -w $PWD fa-repro bash -c '            # bash -c, not bash -lc
export TORCH_BLAS_PREFER_HIPBLASLT=1              # explicit assignment, never setdefault
export HIPBLASLT_TENSILE_LIBPATH=/opt/venv/lib/python3.12/site-packages/_rocm_sdk_libraries_gfx1250/lib/hipblaslt/library/gfx1250   # image library only
export NKFIX_ENABLE=1 NKFIX_CHECK=0               # NKFIX_CHECK=1 for long runs / NaN debugging (+~33 ms/step)
export PYTHONPATH=$TURBO/docs/gfx1250_llama31_8b_e2e/nkfix:$TURBO   # nkfix.py and transpose_triton.py in the same directory
...'
  • Checks:
    • The log must contain [nkfix] installed and must not contain [nkfix] triton transpose unavailable.
    • Steady state is ~24k tps (A0-new-firmware). Settling at ~19.4k means transpose_triton.py is missing; only ~2k means nkfix is not in effect.
  • yaml config: flavor: 8B_flex, converters: ["primus_turbo"], use_turbo_attention: true, compile.enable: false, and set debug.seed.
  • The real fix has to come from the image side: add bf16 tuned libraries for NN/wgrad and fix the library path (0915__opt/VENDOR-REPORT-hipblaslt.md).

Per-call interleaved timing vs. blocked timing (B0, 09-28)For the same code, merely moving its position or changing the combination of same-process arms shifts the reading by 2–10%. r6/beat measured 1.29× interleaved and 1.035× at steady state; bwd beat/current measured 0.7618 interleaved and 0.7878 blockedEvery call inherits the power/clock state left behind by the previous arm, which even a 2 GB flush does not clear, so the result favors the higher-power armSwitched to blocked timing: each arm first runs 4 calls, then times 9, in palindromic order (fwd from 03:40 via h40/h41, bwd from ~05:00 via h66). The conclusion that clock and power were "ruled out" was retracted; several conventions for the throttle cost were mixed upOn 09-24 it was announced that sclk had always been 1100 MHz at 853 W, ruling out throttling. On 09-13 the throttle cost was estimated at 1.65×; on 09-14 this was changed to "measured ~1.93×, 1.65× void". The 09-15 JIRA-TRACE-ANALYSIS (later carried into PROGRESS-REPORT-0917.html and 0922_summary/ASM-ATTENTION.md) said that attention on A0 and B0 differs by only ~13% (A0/B0: fwd 1.118, bwd 1.150, total 1.146), and claimed both sides were "the same ASM backward implementation"pw.sh passed --arms cur_a, benchmark.py exited with exit 2 and its output went to /dev/null, yet wait returned 0, so those 60 lines were actually idle data. The real 264 samples show a prod window of 998–1029 MHz; 1100 is only the idle clock. 1.65× was an estimate from the MAX_CLK ratio, not a measurement. 1.93× is the B0 five-row same-version comparison (total) of 1.88–2.01× (median 1.93×), i.e. the A0-VR→B0 cross-card ratio (B0 itself also has VR warnings, at a cost of ~9%), not A0's own throttle cost; A0's own same-code measurements before vs. after the reflash are op 1.24–1.67× and e2e 1.44× (e2e has additional differences, see Appendix A.2). So 1.65× is not void, only an estimate. The "13%" is wrong in two places: A0's bwd 10.160 is the contaminated shim number (see row 1 of this table); B0's 8.835 is the fused Triton bwd (on 09-14 B0 had no ASM bwd yet; HANDOFF lists it as to-do T2, and the ledger records are all labeled vendored fused backward). fwd 1.118× (clean ASM fwd on both sides) holds; the same-code bwd (fused Triton bwd on both sides) is estimated from the two RESULTS files as 17.735/8.835≈2.01×Measurement scripts must assert that the object under test actually ran (echo RC per shape). d.4 Toolchain problems

Problem Symptom Root cause Solution Cost/lesson Source
Three FlyDSL versions coexistThe image ships 0.2.4 (Primus-Turbo main pins this version, for the gfx950 tree); 0.3.2 is the version pinned by aiter (~/.local/flydsl032, used by the bwd job); 0.3.4.1 lives in ~/.local/flydsl0341 (used by the fwd job and e2e). With 0.3.2 on PYTHONPATH, import primus_turbo fails with cannot import name 'buffer_ops'. The B0 smoke test reports AssertionError: flydsl 0.2.4 @ /opt/venv0.3.x removed flydsl.expr.buffer_ops. Primus's base_env puts the image's site-packages first, and _ensure_flydsl0341() does not move the path to the front once it sees it is already in sys.path. Installing matplotlib with pip --target let numpy 2.5.3 overwrite the 2.4.1 that torch depends on_env.py does sys.path.insert(0, dir) before importing, and asserts the version and __file__; job processes never import primus_turbo. 0.3.2 and 0.3.4.1 perform on par in same-session A/B: fly/asm 0.657–0.664 and 0.664–0.666 respectively (A0-throttled); r29/s6 have byte-identical ISA under both versionsThe Stage 4 dispatch wiring had to be redesigned. API traps on gfx1250Compiles, but goes silently wrong or hangs at run timeis_rdna_arch("gfx1250") returns False: 0.2.4 therefore misjudges it as wave64; in 0.3.x it only affects the V# flags, so OOB_SELECT is missing and the buffer descriptor takes the CDNA form 0x00027000. rocdl.s_waitcnt throws on gfx1250 (the counters are separate). BufferAtomicAdd generates SCOPE_CU, which silently loses updates across the 8 XCDs. V# num_records is in units of 128 B, and int32 arithmetic wraps around to 0 at prod shapes. TDM without pad has 64-way bank conflicts. make_tdm_atom(num_warps=8) used under 4-wave silently misses loads. aiter fwd's buffer manager throws NotImplementedError when num_waves != 8. fastmath=fast let LLVM delete the causal −inf mask. A Python if rewritten into an scf.if branch function raises NameError (needs const_expr). Appending to a list inside a statement-level for range does not work. exec() breaks source introspectionUse UniversalAtomicAdd(Float32, SyncScope.Agent) to get SCOPE_DEV; use a bare fx.barrier() or rocdl.s_wait_dscnt; use explicit predicates and clamping rather than relying on descriptor truncation; compute extents in i64; use aiter's tdm_ops_gfx1250 and pass pad explicitly; record num_waves!=8 as a roadblock rather than as "4-wave is slower"Caused build failures over several rounds; h41 misjudged the atomic scope, corrected by 4e02d3aeskill:env-and-pitfalls §7; skill:flydsl-api.md; 0921__flydsl/A0-A1-ZERO-GPU-COMPILE.md §3

Process management and supervisionThe VENV in supervise_job.sh was hard-coded to someone else's path; when the bin was not found it exited 127 and then restarted forever. MAX_RESTARTS=60, and the backoff only grows, never shrinks. The supervisor only checks whether the process has died, not for wedges or stalls. Killing the loop with kill <pid> leaves orphaned agents that keep writing job_context. An act interrupted by op-evolve stop reruns from scratch next time (B0 bwd r25 ran twice because of this). Before stop takes effect, the framework has already built the empty shell of the next round. The 09-25 resume did not use setsid and kept its logs in /tmp; they were lost after the 10:10:51 reboot. meas2 left over from r23 occupied the card concurrently with validation. On 09-24 the .stop came a few tens of seconds late, and the next round's opt had already startedThe framework monitors only one dimension, "process death"; manual operating habits were wrongUse setsid nohup … resume; stop with op-evolve stop or kill -TERM -<PGID>; deploy card_ok.sh, patrol and release_guard; in monitoring, use a bracket pattern such as [o]p-evolve to avoid matching itself—skill:op-evolve-ops §3 §11; 0917__flydsl/op-evolve/LAUNCHED.md

d.6 Correctness and precision gates

A four-tensor SQNR gate is neededOn 09-13 (A0-throttled), dk/dv = −inf occurred once, while out 53.67 and dq 52.24 dB were both normal (the run at the time was fwd:num_stages=2; bwd:num_warps=2,waves_per_eu=1). This was a one-off event: about 1/100 of runs; 3 immediate repeats all passed, and it did not recur in the following 2,300 calls (1000+1000 in-process, 300 in total across 150 fresh processes); the original write-up judged it "not a property of any config" and suspected a transient hardware fault; but it shows that a gate looking only at out/dq misses errors. In aiter, changing only BLOCK_N1=256 was 1.31× faster, but dq was only 9.59 dB. With dkdv's BLOCK_M changed to 128, the result was "smoothly wrong". When num_block_m did not follow BLOCK_M, the dk/dv tail stayed at the zeros initial value. sequence_parallel=False computed only 1/128A gate that looks only at out cannot see backward errors; the grid and the tiling parameters are coupled to each otherout/dq/dk/dv each ≥50 dB; before dispatch, assert BLOCK_N1==BLOCK_M2 and BLOCK_M1==BLOCK_N2 (rejected 3 configs); prefill outputs with NaN and check isfinite coverage; the fwd gate imposes separate requirements on o and lse (later lowered to 49 dB, see d.5)Wrong configs often look faster0913__opt_plan__claude/phase1/RESULTS.md §4 §12 (:89-95, :380-393); 0913__opt_plan__claude/phase2/DECISIONS.md D1 D4 D8

Poisoning allocator (poison allocator)A candidate's out-of-bounds write clobbered the interrupt ring, yet poison_allocator did not catch the "never-written elements"The largest poison block was only 64 MiB while prod's dq is 256 MiB, so the caching allocator never hands a poison block to dq, and fresh pages are all 0. The fill value was fp32 NaN 0x7FC00000, half of which reads as 0.0 when read as bf16. In our own fix, 0x7FC07FC0−2^32 in turn overflowed int32. In addition, the offline table's mean-SQNR gate lets NaN through (nan < 50 is False)Switched to 0x7FC07FC0 (NaN in all three dtypes), written through an int32 view, with block sizes covering downward from the largest tensor; placed in op/poison_util.py (686c9927) without touching ut/common.py, so that the refcache is not invalidated; prefill with NaN and check isfinite coverage (720 calls, 0 non-finite values)—cba2abc2; 686c9927; 0923__flydsl/poison-fix/README.md; 0915__opt/NAN-FINDING.md:66-73

Thin SQNR margin, low discriminating powerThe SQNR ceiling of bf16 gradients is about 52–53 dB, only about 2.5 dB above the 50 dB gate. ASM bwd's dk/dv are only 50.2–51.0 dB (dk 50.56, dv 50.84 at s4096), because the host side first rounds each of the g=4 partial sums to bf16 and then adds them. armN changed the fp32 accumulation order, yet SQNR still read 52.61/52.65/52.83, showing no difference. In the region skipped by causal masking the true values are close to 0, so an off-by-one or a race could still pass the gateSQNR is a global averageRely mainly on element-wise bitwise comparison: armAB vs. the incumbent had zero differences across 134,217,728 elements. SQNR is only auxiliary; use SQNR fingerprints to audit which implementation actually ranWhen the threshold is raised or the shape changes, ASM will be the first to hit the line0922_summary (xlsx NOTES 4/12); 0925__flydsl/AITER-5GEMM-STUDY.md §2
Finding out-of-bounds accesses by proofOn 09-24 an exhaustive enumeration of prod's 134,217,728 tuples proved that the reads and writes of k_dq and k_dq_sp are all bijections onto the real tensors. It also found 3 latent defects: (1) impl.py:115 asserts only sq%32, while the launcher rounds down and the kernel rounds up, so when sq%64==32 there is an out-of-bounds write of 262,144 B; (2) dqp's num_records is computed in int32 and wraps to 0 when nsp≥8, so all dQ writes are dropped; (3) k_dkdv's prefetch is not clamped and reads up to 982,272 B past the end, stopped only by the real extent. B0 r19 made 1,580 out-of-bounds prefetches across 9 UT shapes (256 on prod)Address-arithmetic changes came without matching bounds proofsOn B0, the three fixes from h33 gave r19h: 0 out-of-bounds accesses, bitwise-identical output, no measurable overhead (1.0001). w4f was re-proved after its proof model was corrected (it had mistaken a partial wait for retirement of the whole TDM batch). Note: do not change k_dq's descriptor to the real extent; that would drop 75% of dQ—41c916b7; 0924__flydsl/kdq-enumeration/SYNTHESIS.md; 0927__b0/lab-bwd-r19/REPORT.md §3–§4
Gates for the product portRegression tests found that the gate let G=6/7 through, and fwd asserted at build time; at G=32, n_seq=0. An adversarial review confirmed 3 more issues: the window check on the apply path, the autotune cache key, and the launch memo under COMPILE_ONLYfwd's O store requires G to divide 32 and be ≤16Gate tightened to G∈{1,2,4,8,16}; added a gqa16 precision test and rejection tests for G=7/32; committed only after the 3 review issues were fixed—s:fdc2534d 09-30T04:37–04:56Z
e2e correctness: the NaN runs were actually the fastest8 of 74 e2e runs had NaN loss, and each was the fastest in its group: fly 49,487 vs. 46.5k (+6.4%), asm-on 44,294 vs. 42.5k, v3 38,713 vs. 37.7k, nkfix2-on 12,101 vs. 11,650Scoring by tps only, never looking at loss; broken computation is often fastere2e.sh counts nan_steps and prints DISCARD; judge correctness first, then speed; if one run goes wrong, re-audit all runs; the watchdog in later e2e runs exits on any non-finite value (exit 96)4 already-reported numbers were retracted (see d.2)PROGRESS-REPORT-0917.html; skill:gfx1250-card-safety §8; 1002__e2e/E2E-PLAN.md

For ASM's own correctness problems, see section (b): out-of-bounds dk/dv writes under GQA (−0.94/−0.65 dB; the workaround is dkdv_heads=q plus a host-side reduction), dq of _perf.co only 5.84 dB, and ASM fwd's LSE layout is incompatible with the two-kernel bwd (mixing them silently computes wrong gradients).

Open or unverified items (section d scope)

  • The direct causes of class-B wedges (starting with INVALIDATE_TLBS) and of the startup-time MES fault family are both still unknown. There is also no usable precursor indicator.
  • The root cause of nkfix producing NaN in 21% of runs on A0 has not been found. Between the host hipBLASLt library and NaN and wedges there is only correlational evidence, apart from the single-variable pair B0 p1a/p1b (n=1 per side; it answers the objection raised in B0 VERIFY); this has not been confirmed repeatedly on the card.
  • The 09-21 16:01:54 wedge was judged unrecoverable by the session, but 4547915c says dmesg had no reset signature at all. The first-line signature of the 09-22 16:02 wedge was not saved. Neither of the two can be assigned a class.
  • In the AC count, the two on 09-11 (AC power cycle or warm reboot) and the 9th on 09-16 (inferred) are uncertain. The reboot times on 09-13 and 10-02 were not recorded.
  • Documents that need correcting:
    • env-and-pitfalls §1b still recommends the host library, which conflicts with h85 and the tutorial.
    • gfx1250-card-safety says "five AC power cycles in one day on 09-16"; it was actually 9.
    • The same skill says "the 09-16 cycle truncated 6 objects"; it was actually 09-17.
    • skill:gfx1250-attn-campaign/SKILL.md:106 and 0930__bwd/PLAN.md D2 say PC sampling "wedged 3/3"; the original record is that all 3 faulted and 1 wedged (the "3 attempts 3 faults" in env-and-pitfalls §2c is accurate).
    • 0930__bwd/PLAN.md D7 and bwd hint h83 say A0 r24 was accepted by "geometric mean"; it was actually the arithmetic mean (20baa45e).
    • 0930__bwd/PLAN.md P4/D1 says the r24–r32 history of the B0 bwd job is unobtainable, and 0930__bwd/REPORT.md:16 says the real dumps exist only on B0; in fact both have been on A0 since 09-28, in /home/lihuzhan/code/0928__bak_b0.tar.gz (see d.7).
    • The 1ee0dd59 commit message, 1002__oe/incident/WEDGE-1002.md, the body of h85 (0930__bwd/oejob/hint.md:4806) and gfx1250-card-safety §1 #13 record the two variants of the 10-02 r27 wedge as w4f fused kernels; per the source in rounds/027/_scratch/arms, they were actually cluster multicast on an s6 base (A_g74 modifies k_dkdv, B_g82 modifies k_dqg), see d.1.1.
  • The gb ruler has been designed but not installed (needs user approval; optionally spend ~6 min of card time first to calibrate the burst length). The magnitude of s6 vs. ASM was measured on 10-02 (A0-new-firmware, real data): blk 0.972, gb 1.038, and an in-e2e attention bwd per-step time ratio of 0.974 (p1: 162.15 / 166.54 ms; this is the bwd time ratio, not the whole-step ratio. bwd is ~4 ms faster than ASM per step: adjacent-pair differences 4.50 / 4.56 ms, i.e. the 4.5 in the main text of 1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md; subtracting the two arms' medians gives 3.8–4.4 ms), so gb is pessimistic by ~6% on this item.
  • In the same e2e, fly's attention total (fwd+bwd) per step was ~4.3–5.2 ms more than ASM's (fwd 43.9 vs. 34.7, +9.2; bwd ~−4), yet the whole step was 0.7–2.0 ms faster (1,349.4 / 1,349.9 vs. 1,350.6). In both processes step − FA is −6.3 ms, beyond the 5 ms threshold of criterion 2 in E2E-PLAN §4; the same holds for s6 vs. r29 (attention −53.0 ms, while the step is only 37.0–39.1 ms faster). The difference comes from parts outside attention or from noise, and has not been broken down [1002__e2e/e2e/runs/analysis.1002_095504.txt].
  • In e2e, ASM per layer (fwd 1.07–1.11 ms, bwd 5.11–5.29 ms) is shorter than what either of the two op rulers measures, which the clock cannot explain; the hypothesis that "the inputs are hot in MALL" was not measured (RULER §8.1).
  • Two documents disagree on L21's real gain: REPORT-0928.html says +0.3%, while the blocked reading in ruler/REPORT.md is +0.6%.

Appendix A Conventions and comparability

A.1 Machines, clock stages and driver/firmware

The four sections name the same stage differently: (a) uses A0-VR / A0-RF / B0, (b) uses E1–E5, (c) uses A0-H / A0-T / B0-14 / B0-28 / A0-R / A0-D, and (d) uses A0-throttled / A0-new-firmware / B0. The mapping is as follows.

Three flydsl versions coexist:

  • 0.3.2: the version pinned by aiter (~/.local/flydsl032); used by the A0 bwd job and the hand-campaign s1–s6;
  • 0.3.4.1: ~/.local/flydsl0341; used by the fwd job, B0 and e2e from 09-25 on.

A.3 FLOP conventions

  • Current convention (op-evolve tools/op_flops.py): per call, fwd 2.199292e12 and bwd 5.498229e12 (5-GEMM nominal count, causal taken as (s+1)/(2s)), fwd+bwd 7.697e12; ms = FLOP ÷ TF/s. Values marked * in the (b) tables and labeled derived in the (c) tables are all converted this way.
  • Documents from 09-13..09-16: report only the fwd+bwd total (7.697e12 ÷ total time). The per-direction TF/s in the (a)(b) tables were converted from the single-direction times.
  • 7-GEMM issued convention (not used in this report): the in-tree vendored fused bwd issues 7 GEMM passes, issued 7.6975e12, 1.400× the nominal; our FlyDSL bwd (r0 to s6) issues 7.7395e12, 1.40764× the nominal (including the diagonal-block granularity ×1.00546); aiter ASM issues only 1.0155× the nominal. So s6's "103.8% of ASM" is achieved while doing ~39% more matrix math. The 0913/0914 documents at one point reported bwd on the issued convention (e.g. B0 vendored fused bwd 748.4 vs 534.6 TF/s nominal).
  • Other old conventions: 09-23 STAGE2-FWD-SWEEP used fwd ≈2.233e12 (old harness, TF/s ~1.5% too high); the 5.8 TF/s in 09-17 DKDV-FIRST-TIMING is a single-head custom "useful" convention and not comparable; the 09-14 B0 Triton op-evolve job counts bwd only.
  • GEMM: TF/s = 2MNK ÷ time.
  • e2e: tokens/s is single-card throughput; TFLOP/s is torchtitan's self-reported value, or is converted from tps on torchtitan's convention (32 layers ~57.9 GFLOP/token, 8 layers ~16.84); torchtitan's MFU uses the A100's 312 TF/s as its denominator and is entirely unusable.
  • Ratio direction: "relative to ASM" in (b) = this row's time ÷ ASM time (>1 means slower than ASM); "% of ASM" in (c) = ASM time ÷ this row's time (>100% means faster than ASM). The two are reciprocals of each other; take care when quoting them.

A.4 Rulers
