A.5 Noise floor

  • op: the same-session noise floor of this op is 0.24–0.66% (before 09-24 the 1.57% of the HipKittens GEMM ladder was mistakenly used); fwd cross-session drift ~1.5%; the same r7 code read 382.73 → 404.06 TF/s (+5.6%) before vs after an AC power cycle; blocked A/A is fwd ±0.19%, bwd ±0.05%; the acceptance min_gain is 0.007 (0 for bwd early on).
  • fast shape: ~55 µs per call, dominated by launch overhead. Two arms running the same kernel differ by only 0.05% in min, but can differ by 56% in median, so fast was later switched to scoring by min, with its weight set to 0.
  • e2e: on A0-VR, before nkfix the results were trimodal, with cross-run sd 3.91% (between clusters 1.8% / 6.8%); after nkfix sd 0.42%; on B0 one process stepped down as a whole by ~5% midway (2,015 → 1,918 tps, cause not investigated); cutting the step count from 20 to 10 changed the median of steps 4–8 by ≤0.22%.
  • Card contention: on B0 09-14, with all four cards fully loaded, per-card timing drifted by up to 50%; sustained GEMMs on a neighboring card make attention on the card under test 3–11× slower.

A.6 Inconsistencies between sections: reconciled and unresolved

The first version listed here 11 records from the four sections that contradicted each other. For the ones that could be verified, this version corrected the main text and lists them under "Reconciled" (if an old statement still lingers somewhere, this section prevails); the rest are either not yet verified or are differences in convention that do not amount to contradictions, and are listed under "Unresolved or different conventions".

Reconciled

  • torchtitan version of the A0 09-13 e2e: v0.2.2 (Primus third_party/torchtitan pinned at 73a0e6979). The "0.1.0" written in 0914__campaign/RESULTS.md:65 is a typo.
  • "Measured VR throttle cost ~1.93×": 1.88–2.01× (median 1.93×) is the A0-VR→B0 cross-card ratio (B0 itself also has a ~9% VR cost), not A0's own throttle cost. A0's own before/after-reflash factors are op 1.24–1.67× and e2e 1.44× (the latter with additional differences). The 09-13 1.65× is a clock-ratio estimate and is no longer called void (see A.2).
  • "ASM attention differs across machines by only 1.12–1.15×": only the fwd 1.118× (1.572 vs 1.406 ms) holds; the bwd item compares the shim artifact 10.160 ms with fused Triton 8.835 ms and does not hold (see A.2, d.3).
  • The 220.6 TF/s in the c1325c7e commit message: measurement conditions unknown (the commit message states neither hkv, nor whether causal, nor the clock); which clock ratio 220.6 / 129.4 = 1.70× matches is undetermined. This report no longer says "it was measured on an unthrottled card".
  • AC count: d.1.4 is authoritative. A0 had 27 manual power cycles/reboots because of wedges, 28 counting the ineffective AC on 09-29; of these, five were on 09-15 and nine on 09-16 (UTC).
  • FlyDSL fwd speedup after reflash: 1.47–1.50×; order o1 2.023→1.353 ms is 1.50×, order o2 1.992→1.356 ms is 1.47×, mean ~1.48× (0928__a0_repro/REPORT.md §6.1).

Unresolved or different conventions

  1. Source of the 09-14 B0 "turbo vs flex 1.459×": the retraction note in (b) b.3 says "the difference comes from other turbo patches (float8 / mx linear)"; (a) a.3 #2 and #11 consider this attribution doubtful (going by the Primus code on this machine, it is not apparent how these two patches would take effect under converters: []); all that is certain is that it does not come from attention. Unresolved.
  2. aiter Triton MHA factory-default readings (09-13 34.565 ms vs 09-15 28.035 ms): (b) b.2.1 cites the 0915 assessment that the 09-13 row is off by +23% and unreliable; (a) a.2 #12 considers the 09-15 run the more likely anomaly (its fwd 3.725 ms looks like a tuned forward). Not re-measured; unresolved.
  3. B0 clock during training: (b) E4 gives 1.25–1.45 GHz, (c) B0-28 gives 1.34–1.39 GHz, (d) gives 1.25–1.69 GHz. The former lean toward the whole-step sclk mean, the latter is a reading of the attention window during training; the conventions differ, and they have not been cross-checked item by item.
  4. A0-VR loaded clock: the sections give 0.94 / 0.95 / 0.97–1.07 GHz respectively, plus a single-point sample of 943 MHz. All refer to the loaded clock under the VR throttle; the differences come from different measurement windows.
  5. Real gain of L21: REPORT-0928.html says +0.3%, the blocked reading in ruler/REPORT.md is +0.6% (already listed at the end of (d)). Unresolved.
  6. 10-02 e2e fwd/bwd split (wording reconciled, one item still not broken down): "bwd faster by 4.5 ms per step" in the main text of 1002__e2e/RESULT-e2e.md is the median of adjacent paired differences (−4.50 / −4.56 ms in the two processes); subtracting the two arms' own medians gives 3.8–4.4 ms (166.54−162.15, 166.41−162.62; 3.9–4.3 after rounding in the RESULT-e2e.md table). The two do not contradict each other, and this report uniformly writes "~4 ms". What is still not broken down: fly's attention total per step is ~4.3–5.2 ms more than ASM's, yet the step is 0.7–2.0 ms faster; step − FA is −6.3 ms in both processes, beyond the 5 ms threshold of criterion 2 in E2E-PLAN §4; the same holds for s6 vs r29 (attention −53.0 ms, while the step is only 37.0–39.1 ms faster). This difference lies outside attention or in timing noise [1002__e2e/e2e/runs/analysis.1002_095504.txt].

Appendix B Glossary

Term Meaning See
A0 / B0A0 = heliosr-1b114-c07-1 (this machine, single gfx1250 card); B0 = ctheliosp-1b112-a37-1 (4× gfx1250, same XGMI hive)Appendix A.1
Clock-stage codesA0-VR / A0-T / A0-throttled / E1·E3·E3′ = A0 before the firmware reflash; A0-RF / A0-R / A0-new-firmware / E5 = after the reflash; A0-D = after the 10-01 driver change; E2 / B0-14 = B0 09-14; E4 / B0-28 = B0 09-27/28Appendix A.1
VR throttleDuring 09-10..09-28, A0 printed GPU is throttled ... VR at every amdgpu init, and sclk DPM was left with only 500/1100 MHz; lifted by the VBIOS/SMU reflash on 09-29(a) a.1
ASM (aiter ASM)The assembly kernels (.co) that aiter precompiles for gfx1250. fwd is fmha_bf16_pertokenBf16_hd128_128x256_mask.co (entry fmha_fwd_with_sink_asm); bwd is three kernels, odo → bwd_hd128_bf16_causal_br_a32_pssk → dq_convert. The reference for every "relative to ASM / % of ASM" figure in this project(b) b.1, b.4
beat / barbeat: the aiter ASM arm measured in the same session as the candidate inside op-evolve; bar: a quoted ASM reference value (A0-VR 09-24 census: fwd 1.5724 ms, bwd 7.6766 ms; for the bar of each stage see (c) c.1)(c) c.1
10.160 ms (shim artifact)ASM bwd measured through the autograd shim on 09-15: every call did 3 hipModuleLoad calls and freshly allocated ~1 GiB of scratch, plus ~1.4 ms of autograd overhead. Retracted on 09-24; the real bar is 7.6766 ms(b) b.4; (d) d.3
GQA out-of-bounds write / dkdv_heads=qThe grid of aiter's gfx1250 ASM bwd is (kv_tiles, nhead_q, batch), yet it indexes the kv-sized dk/dv by q head, so with G=4, 4 WGs write the same tile without synchronization. The workaround is to allocate dk/dv per q head and then reduce on the host side(a) a.2 #13; (b) b.4
7-GEMM / 5-GEMMTo keep dq deterministic (no atomics), our 1-wave bwd has to issue 7 GEMM equivalents, while aiter ASM uses atomic accumulation for dq and issues only 5; structural factor 1.386–1.408×(b) b.4; Appendix A.3
SQNR gateSignal-to-noise ratio of the output against the fp32 reference. Each of the four tensors out/dq/dk/dv must reach ≥50 dB (the fwd gate was later lowered to 49 dB); dk/dv must be bitwise identical across 200 runs, and from 09-25 on dq instead requires run-to-run ≥70 dB(d) d.5, d.6
nkfixA runtime patch in e2e that sidesteps the bad hipBLASLt tile: it intercepts aten::mm with TorchDispatchMode and rewrites the operands of backward GEMMs into the physical layout of the forward ones (dgrad: mm(A, B.t().contiguous().t()); wgrad: mm(A.contiguous(), B.t().contiguous().t()); the wgrad criterion must be checked before the dgrad one), so that they hit MT256x256x128 instead of the GEMV tile MT32x16x32. Versions: A0 v1 (09-15, dgrad only); v3 (09-16, adds wgrad; rule 3 routes wgrad to an offline FlyDSL GEMM table, off by default); B0 nkfix_b0.py (rewritten 09-28: Triton blocked transpose transpose_triton.py, blocking along the output dimension, NKFIX_CHECK non-finite check; the tutorial uses this version). Overhead ~70 ms per step; NKFIX_CHECK=1 adds another ~33 ms(d) d.2.2
MT32x16x32 / MT256x256x128Macro tiles of hipBLASLt (Tensile) kernels. The former is a small tile meant for GEMV; backward GEMMs that land on it reach only 50–80 TF/s. The latter is the large tile that all of them hit after nkfix(d) d.2
refcachePrecompute on the CPU and cache the fp32 references used by the precision gate and the benchmark (prod 10m26s, 1.1 GB), so that fp32 Tensile references are never run on the card (the source of class A wedges); a sha mismatch is judged FAIL outright(d) d.1.3, d.5
prod / proxy / fastThe three scoring shapes of op-evolve (B, Sq, Skv, Hq, Hkv, D): prod (4,8192,8192,32,8,128), i.e. the Llama-3.1-8B training shape; proxy (1,4096,4096,32,8,128); fast (1,1024,1024,8,2,128). Ranking is by prod only (h7); fast is launch-bound, and its weight was later set to 0(c) c.1
Per-call interleaved rulerThe original timing method of op-evolve benchmark.py: the arms alternate call by call in palindromic order, median of 51 calls per arm. Each call inherits the power/clock state left behind by the previous arm, so the FlyDSL/ASM ratio is systematically biased low (fwd ~25%, bwd ~3.4%)(d) d.3
Blocked ruler (blk)The timing method used from 09-28 on (h40/h41/h66): each arm first runs 4 untimed calls, then 9 consecutive timed calls, with palindromic order across blocks; every ranking process carries an A/A copy (fwd ±0.19%, bwd ±0.05%), and an A/A deviation above 0.5% voids the whole process. blk also denotes "a reading under the blocked ruler", as opposed to gb(c) c.1; (d) d.3
Real-data ruler (real-dump ruler)Replaces randn with the real q/k/v of 6 layers dumped at B0 training step 43 (h45/h47). The real score standard deviation is 21–53; for randn it is ~1(d) d.3
Training operating point (training clock) / gb rulerDuring training, the GEMMs max out the board power, and attention runs at a much lower clock than in op tests (1.25–1.69 GHz on B0 during training; ~1.28 GHz on A0 after a GEMM burst). Before each timing, the gb ruler first runs 10 bf16 32768×4096×14336 GEMMs to reproduce this operating point. ASM barely changes with the clock, while FlyDSL slows down. gb has been designed but is not yet installed in op-evolve scoring(d) d.3; 1002__oe/RULER.md
wedge (GPU wedge) classes A/B/COnce the card is wedged, the driver cannot complete a reset, and only an AC power cycle recovers it. Classified by the first dmesg line: A is an out-of-bounds address write by the on-card fp32 Tensile reference GEMM (truncated sub-4GB address, PF 0x5 / RW 0x1); B has an INVALIDATE_TLBS timeout as its first line, with no memory fault of any kind before it; C is a GC_UTCL2 fault storm set off by an out-of-bounds read in a candidate kernel, accompanied by IH ring buffer overflow. There are also S (startup-time MES fault family), K (known operational trigger), R (resource/process), F (process-level recoverable fault, not counted as a wedge), D (degraded) and X (non-kernel causes such as driver/firmware)(d) d.1.1
AC cycle (AC power cycle)The user, in person at the machine, cuts power to the whole machine and powers it back on; this is the only recovery path after a wedge (modprobe -r amdgpu makes the whole machine unreachable). After recovery: sudo modprobe amdgpu, set dmesg_restrict back to 0, confirm that KFD is empty, git fsck(d) d.1.3
MES / KFDMES is the hardware-scheduler microcode on the GPU; wedge signatures are mostly MES failed to respond, wait for reset ack. KFD is the /dev/kfd compute driver interface; after a wedge, the processes' references to it never drop to zero(d) d.1
TDMTensor Data Mover: gfx1250's asynchronous tensor-copy unit, which moves global data into LDS according to a descriptor; in FlyDSL it corresponds to make_tdm_atom / tdm_ops. The main lever of s3–s6 is precisely "3-stage TDM LDS ring + reading the next iteration's B operand back into registers ahead of time"(c) #73, #76
WMMAThe matrix multiply-accumulate instruction of gfx1250 (wave32), replacing CDNA's MFMA. The roofline study measured 8 cycles per WMMA and ~29 cycles per WMMA↔DS switch(c) #69; 0930__roofline/REPORT.md
MFMA / ds_read_tr16_b64 / permlane32_swapCDNA (gfx950)-only instructions; on gfx1250 all of them fail with "Cannot select", so the gfx950 FlyDSL / HipKittens attention cannot be ported over(a) a.2 #6; (b) b.2.3
ATTrocprofv3's thread trace, which gives per-instruction Hitcount / Latency / Stall. On A0 it became usable for FlyDSL JIT and ASM .co only after the 09-29 firmware reflash(d) d.4
ITTTRITON_HIP_USE_IN_THREAD_TRANSPOSE, a Triton compile switch found by op-evolve on 09-14 that makes the fused bwd ~9.7% faster (a009599b)(c) #13, #14
k_dkdv / k_dqgOur FlyDSL bwd consists of several kernels: k_delta (odo preprocessing); k_dkdv (computes dK/dV per KV tile, with the GQA reduction done in registers, no atomics); k_dq (computes dQ; in the r29/s6 line, proxy/prod shapes go through k_dqg and toy/fast shapes through the split-K version k_dq_sp); k_redsp is the split-K reduction kernel. From s1 on, k_dqg runs on a side stream, concurrently with k_dkdv(c) #26, #38, #72
r20Champion of the A0-VR bwd job (k_dkdv's Q/dO prefetch depth 1→2), 511.42 TF/s; also the starting point of B0 bwd(c) #42
r19hr19 plus the three clamps of h33 (out-of-bounds prefetches 1,580→0, VGPR 904→724), promoted by refactor h68 in B0 r28(c) #61
u2n×2 unroll of k_dq's full-KV loop, removing the register rotation of 65 v_mov_b64 on the back edge (k_dq 3.185→2.990 ms, h70). After A0's firmware reflash it became a net loss instead, so the s series turned it off from c1 on (DQ_U2=False, −0.85%)(c) #62, #70
r29B0 bwd champion = r19h + u2n + g86 (dK/dV epilogue through an LDS ring), 656.5 TF/s on B0 (80.4% of ASM), 83.6% of ASM on A0 after the firmware reflash. It is the bwd in the product branch(c) #62, #69
r13ns / r16Turning off speculative softmax (SPEC_STALE_MAX=False) in the round-13 kernel of the B0 fwd job gives r13ns, which refactor h44 promoted to champion in round 16, so r16 = r13ns. It is the current fwd champion and also the fwd in the product branch(c) #58
s1–s6 / s6Stage champions of the 09-30 A0 hand campaign: s1 = tailpf + trorder + streams; s2 = s1 + soffset; s3 = 3-stage TDM ring for k_dkdv (dkdv_tdm3) + dqg_ts + side stream; s4 = trimmed VALU, v_nop and address computation, with division removed from the counters; s5 = k_dqg also goes through a TDM ring (dqg_tdm); s6 = s5 + VALU trim in k_dqg. s6 runs at 5.295–5.300 ms, 103.8% of ASM, and is the current bwd champion (flydsl 0.3.2; the ISA is byte-for-byte identical under 0.3.4.1); not yet in the product branch(c) #70–#77
w4f4-wave fused bwd: 5 GEMMs, split barrier, dQ by fp32 SCOPE_DEV atomic accumulation. The results are correct, but it is limited by chip-wide atomic throughput: best 6.509 ms (r4b, 09-30), 6.486 ms in A0 r26; relax_noatom, which skips the dQ atomics, gives an upper bound of 4.241 ms (wrong output, used only as a bound). Paused. The two variants that wedged the card in r27 on 10-02 are often misrecorded as w4f; they are actually cluster multicast on an s6 base (A_g74, B_g82)(c) #75, #84; (d) d.1.1
h-hints (hNN)Rules or instructions that the operator writes for the op-evolve agent, kept in the job's hint.md and numbered h<N>. The parser reads only index-table rows whose first cell is h<N>, so h83, which lacked an index row, did not take effect until 10-02. Numbering is per file: hints with the same number in different files are not the same hint (e.g. h41 in A0 bwd is about atomic scope, while h41 in B0 is about blocked timing). File locations: A0 bwd in 0923__flydsl/hint.md, A0 fwd in 0925__flydsl/fwd-job/hint.md, B0 in 0927__b0/{fwd,bwd}-hint.md, A0 after 09-30 (h83/h85) in the bwd job's job_context/hint.md. Frequently cited: h7 (rank by prod only), h18 (aiter's dq atomics cannot pass the determinism gate), h28 (beat in a separate process), h40/h41/h66 (blocked timing), h44 (adopt r13ns), h46/h72 (the FlyDSL JIT cache key misses module constants), h47 (real-data ruler), h50 (no fp32 references computed on the card), h68/h70 (r19h, u2n), h69 (proxy and prod each ≥ beat), h83 (fast scored by min, gain_weights 1/0.25/0), h85 (new kernels go to toy first, then prod; read VGPR counts only via compile-only; use only the image's hipBLASLt)(d) Reading conventions, d.5
op-evolveAn automated, continuous op-optimization framework developed by a colleague (/home/lihuzhan/code/2026_0910__op-evolve/op-evolve). A job advances round by round; the ledgers are in artifacts/<job>/rounds/NNN/ and job_context/state.yaml. A regular (fast) round is 1-opt (the agent proposes and implements a candidate) → 2-reflect; a deep round is 1-profiling → 2-plan → 3-act → 4-reflect; the operator's refactors are recorded in 0-refactor. gain is the ratio between this round's code and the historical best after both are re-measured in the same session; acceptance is judged by min_gain (0.007) and gain_weights; FastError means that the whole round is recorded as failed when an idea was not written into facts/dead_ends(c) c.1; (d) d.5
championA job's currently accepted best code (best_round in state.yaml; the code is in op/current). Note that the per-shape champions pointers in state.yaml can record the best-ever reading of a rejected round, which is not necessarily the same as best_round, and on resume they block later rounds(c) c.1; (d) d.5
